# 90 — Decision Log

Every deliberate departure from OneCLI (`../onecli-spec/docs`) and every
refinement of the proposals (`../onecli-spec/proposals`). New decisions are
appended; superseded ones are marked, never deleted.

| Id | Decision | Instead of | Rationale |
|----|----------|------------|-----------|
| D-01 | All services, CLI, supervisor, connectors and plugin SDK in **Rust**; dashboard in Next.js. | TS control plane/runner/supervisor/adapter + Rust gateway | One language lets crates be shared (policy, protocol, crypto), removing cross-runtime parity suites. User requirement. |
| D-02 | **One policy implementation** (`aurora-policy`) linked by API and gateway. | Proposal 01's API→gateway dry-run endpoint | With one language, linking the crate is simpler and faster than a network dry-run; the gateway still offers `check-snapshot` for plugin tests. Same goal (no second matcher). |
| D-03 | **Postgres `LISTEN/NOTIFY`** is the event bus; claims with `SKIP LOCKED` are the queue. | In-process emitter (API limited to one replica) | Horizontal API scaling without adding Redis/a broker. Notifications are hints; state is re-read. |
| D-04 | **Approvals stored in Postgres**; gateway waits on NOTIFY with polling fallback. | Memory or Redis approval store | Multi-replica gateways without Redis; holds are a slow path so DB writes are acceptable; the request hot path still never touches the DB. |
| D-05 | Rate-limit counters stay **in memory per gateway replica** in phase 0 (documented N× approximation). | Redis counters | Keeps the hot path DB-free with no new dependency; a `CounterStore` seam is reserved. |
| D-06 | Gateway **control API on a separate internal port** (4103), unreachable from sandboxes; no browser → gateway path. | Same port as the proxy, exposed as `/gw/*` | Reduces attack surface. With approvals in Postgres and no vault pairing, browsers never need the gateway. |
| D-07 | Gateway cache invalidation via NOTIFY. | API → gateway HTTP call | Reaches every replica; no API→gateway coupling. |
| D-08 | **Credential selection before the policy decision** (ids only, nothing decrypted). | Decision, then selection, then re-evaluation for account-specific rules | Rules can name connections directly; one evaluation; "policy before decryption" still holds. |
| D-09 | Hand-authored rules (published) are evaluated **before** grant-derived rules within a scope; derived rules are always live (not drafted). | Interleaved derived rules re-materialized into drafts | Admin overrides beat per-agent defaults; grant changes take effect immediately without a publish step. |
| D-10 | Rate-limited is **stricter than needs-approval** when combining scopes. | OneCLI order block < approval < rate-limited < allow | An exhausted quota is a refusal now; asking a human to approve a request that will be rate-limited anyway is pointless. |
| D-11 | Gateway refuses **ambiguous paths** (dot segments, encoded slashes). | Not specified | Prevents matcher/upstream disagreement (path confusion). |
| D-12 | **Pluggable secret stores** with `SecretRef` columns everywhere; the DB row is the compare-and-set authority; stores only append versions. Phase 0: `inline`; phase 3: HashiCorp Vault. | Inline ciphertext / KMS envelope + Bitwarden/1Password vault fallbacks | User requirement (Vault). Version-append stores need no CAS of their own; one refresh discipline for all stores. Bitwarden/1Password pairing flows dropped (could return as stores). |
| D-13 | Inline envelope with **key ids and AAD binding** (purpose, org, owner). | 3-part `iv:tag:ciphertext` without AAD | Key rotation without downtime; ciphertext cannot be swapped between rows. |
| D-14 | **All tokens hashed at rest**; hosted agents have no long-lived token: **boot tokens** per sandbox boot. | Stored agent tokens reused across boots | A leaked sandbox token dies with its boot; nothing re-showable is stored. |
| D-15 | Per-turn **context credentials** (Proposal 02 §10) adopted in phase 0, delivered via per-session **loopback proxies** in the supervisor. | — | Exact approval attribution; proxy env carries no credentials. |
| D-16 | **LLM provider registry** with operator-configured endpoints; phase 0 providers **LiteLLM** (first) and **Anthropic**; API dialects decide harness compatibility. | `anthropic`/`openai` hard-wired into secret types | User requirement; LiteLLM is self-hosted with a configurable host, so endpoints are configuration and LLM hosts are reserved dynamically. |
| D-17 | Phase 0 real harness: **Claude Code** (via LiteLLM's Anthropic-compatible endpoint or Anthropic directly), plus `fake`. | jcode | Mature headless agent runtime that speaks the Anthropic Messages dialect served by both phase 0 providers. Additional adapters later. |
| D-18 | Default hosted agent **`Daneel`** in every new workspace; shared bots default to the same name. | No default agent | User requirement; gives a working first-run path. |
| D-19 | **No editions.** Roles, OIDC SSO and app availability are plain features; SCIM, groups, budgets, billing, desktop takeover deferred or dropped. | Cloud/Community/Enterprise split | Single self-hosted product. |
| D-20 | **Instance admin** flag for deployment-level operations (plugins, keys, connectors, runners, host claims, instance OAuth clients). | Operator via env/installer only | Proposals require operator approvals in the dashboard; org roles must not grant deployment powers. |
| D-21 | Sign-up default **`invite_only`**, with `open` and `sso_jit` modes. | Always-open sign-up creating fenced orgs | Self-hosted team deployments usually restrict registration; JIT serves SSO. |
| D-22 | **Instance-level OAuth clients** stored in the database (in addition to org/workspace BYOC). | Env-var default clients | Both API and gateway need client credentials for refresh; DB is the single source. |
| D-23 | Hook host **pulls** snapshots and modules from the API's internal port; it has no DB credentials. | (Proposal 01 unspecified) | Strengthens the second isolation layer. |
| D-24 | Hook wall time 15 s for connect/mint hooks, 1 s for summarize. | "a few seconds" | Network hooks need realistic budgets; summarize must not delay holds. |
| D-25 | Connector **integration-level ownership** (one replica serves all sockets of an integration). | Presence-level ownership leases | Simpler, matches how platform connections are grouped; balancing by claim count. |
| D-26 | Connector manifests are **signed**; the core verifies the manifest signature; image signatures are left to deployment tooling. | "Connector images are signed" | The core cannot observe the image; it can verify what it receives. |
| D-27 | **Mirror rule**: answers go back through the door the question came in; doorless turns go to the conversation's most recent thread; web-originated turns are not mirrored. | Adapter mirror pass (unspecified for web turns) | Avoids noisy duplication between dashboard and chat while keeping "one conversation, several doors". |
| D-28 | Kubernetes sandboxes are **bare Pods** (`restartPolicy: Never`) + PVC + per-boot Secret. | Job + PVC | The platform, not the kubelet, decides restarts; bootstrap tokens are single use. |
| D-29 | Sandboxes have **no DNS** and a **read-only root filesystem**; boot material arrives as a secret env/Secret consumed by a non-dumpable supervisor. | Internal network with DNS; files mounted | Closes DNS exfiltration; keeps tokens out of the agent-visible environment. |
| D-30 | **podman** backend (Docker-compatible API) for development and single host. | Docker | User requirement; rootless-friendly. |
| D-31 | Single external origin with an edge proxy in **every** deployment (Caddy in compose; phase 1, OPS-017: the chart's Ingress/HTTPRoute, ingress-nginx on the kind dev cluster). | Ports mode vs proxy mode | One routing model, first-party cookies, no CORS. |
| D-32 | Dashboard is a **pure API client** (generated OpenAPI client). | Server actions calling services directly | Removes the "inject providers in every entry point" bug class. |
| D-33 | Platform tools come from an API **tool registry** whose catalogue is synced to the sandbox. | Central switch + hard-coded supervisor list | Adding a tool is an API-only change. |
| D-34 | Webhook verification secrets stored via SecretRef; webhook payloads labelled untrusted in turn text. | Not specified | Consistency with secret storage and prompt-injection hygiene. |
| D-35 | One error envelope `{error:{code,message,details}}` everywhere, including gateway guidance. | Two API shapes + flat gateway shape | Simpler clients; agents read either equally well. |
| D-36 | Approval timeout **300 s** everywhere. | 180 s (gateway) vs 300 s (SDK/docs) | Removes OneCLI's inconsistency. |
| D-37 | Schedule tools renamed `schedule_*`; peer-task tools `*_peer_task`. | `schedule_task`/`list_tasks` overlapping with peer tasks | Avoids ambiguous tool names. |
| D-38 | Messaging `send_as_me`/read-as-user dropped from phase 0. | Slack-read [EE] | Requires `user_tokens`; no phase 0 connector provides it. |
| D-39 | **LLM governance by key origin**: endpoints carry key-origin rules (operator endpoint, LiteLLM team, provider account); keys are verified at save, before first use of each value, and against every response. Users may hold their own keys if they belong to the approved account. | Restricting who may store keys | The requirement is contractual (corporate accounts carry the privacy terms), not about who holds the key. Anthropic reports the key's organization and workspace in response headers, so verification needs no admin credential and sends no prompt. |
| D-40 | **Layered governance that only tightens**: instance ceiling (config) → org governance (live document) → workspace policy → grants; LLM governance is a dedicated gateway step, not rules. | Compiling governance into derived rules | Rules can be reordered and loosened by authors; governance must not be. A dedicated step also gives precise error codes. |
| D-41 | **Optional uncredentialed default-deny** (`credentialed_only` egress posture) with org and workspace allow-lists, applying to the open lane too. | OneCLI: default-deny only for credentialed traffic; open lane never default-denies | The only complete control against exfiltration and against models reached through unrecognized hosts; optional because it requires curating allow-lists. |
| D-42 | Governed LLM hosts (endpoint hosts + known LLM hosts) can never be targeted by generic secrets or app connections. | Plugins barred only from endpoint hosts | Closes the "personal key as a custom secret" bypass. |

## Implementation decisions (orchestrator, 2026-11-05)

Decisions made while implementing the spec. They do not change product
behaviour; they record environment and tooling choices.

| Id | Decision | Rationale |
|----|----------|-----------|
| I-01 | Requirement traceability ids are `M<n>/<doc>§<section>` (milestone + owning section, e.g. `M0/03§1`) plus the `SEC-nn` invariants of `17`. | The spec has no per-requirement ids; milestones in `19` define acceptance. |
| I-02 | Local toolchain is the system Rust (Arch, 1.99 stable, no rustup). `rust-toolchain.toml` pins `stable` + `wasm32-wasip2` for CI/rustup users, but the **wasm32-wasip2 target is not installed locally**: `aurora-hook-wit`, `aurora-plugin-sdk` and wasm fixture hooks join the workspace at M3 and need the target (rustup or the distro wasm target package). | Environment fact; blocks only M3 hook builds. |
| I-03 | `profile.dev` uses `debug = "line-tables-only"` and no debuginfo for dependencies; one shared `target/`. | Dev host has ~39 GB free disk; wasmtime/aws-lc-rs/kube debug builds are large. |
| I-04 | Version set pinned to the known-compatible line: axum 0.8, utoipa 5 + utoipa-axum 0.2, sqlx 0.8 (+ sqlx-cli 0.8.6), hyper 1, rustls 0.23 (aws-lc-rs), schemars 1. | Mutually compatible; newer majors (sqlx 0.9, utoipa 6) can be adopted later in one task. |
| I-05 | sqlx compile-time checked macros run **online** against the dev database (`DATABASE_URL` in the gitignored `.env`, dev Postgres container `aurora-dev-pg` on `127.0.0.1:55432`); `cargo sqlx prepare --workspace` keeps `.sqlx/` committed so CI builds offline (`SQLX_OFFLINE=true`). | Spec pins compile-time checked queries; online locally, offline in CI. |
| I-06 | Database tests use `#[sqlx::test(migrator = "aurora_db::MIGRATOR")]` (fresh database per test on the server named by `DATABASE_URL`); `aurora-testkit` wraps it and can start a podman Postgres when no server is configured. | Meets "one database per test" without bespoke harness code. |
| I-07 | HTTP error envelope rendering for axum lives behind the `axum` cargo feature of `aurora-core` (the type itself is dependency-light). | Keeps `aurora-core` usable from non-HTTP crates without adding a crate outside the `02` layout. |
| I-08 | `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` indexes `05` (owning document for routes) and records the response/request **schemas** that `05` leaves implicit, defined per milestone before parallel backend/frontend work. The generated OpenAPI document (`/v1/openapi.json`, checked in as `web/openapi.json`) is the machine contract the dashboard client is generated from. | One source per fact; frontend never hand-writes API types. |
| I-09 | Shared service bootstrap (JSON tracing with `service`/`instance`, Prometheus `/metrics`, health router, graceful shutdown) lives in `aurora-core` behind feature `service`. Health/metrics listeners: api 4101, gateway 4103 only (never the proxy port), hookhost 4104, runner ops port **4106** (not the sandbox-facing 4105), fake connector 4190. | Avoids six copies of bootstrap code without adding a crate; keeps metrics off agent-reachable ports (SEC-09 spirit). |

## Spec fixes (contradictions found during implementation)

| Id | Fix | Source of contradiction |
|----|-----|-------------------------|
| S-01 | `org_app_availability`: primary key `(org_id, app_id)` replaced by `UNIQUE NULLS NOT DISTINCT (org_id, app_id, workspace_id)`. | `07 §App availability` needs `(org, app, NULL)` and `(org, app, workspace)` rows simultaneously; the PK allowed one row per app. |
| S-02 | Org-scope partial uniques with nullable `workspace_id` (e.g. `policy_generations` one draft/published per scope) use `NULLS NOT DISTINCT`. | `03` states the uniques but NULL workspace ids would never collide under default semantics. |

### Implementation decisions (continued)

| Id | Decision | Rationale |
|----|----------|-----------|
| I-10 | SMTP delivery uses `lettre` (tokio + rustls, no native-tls); unset `email.smtp_url` ⇒ log-only mailer (`04 §Email and password`). | `02` pins no mail library; lettre is the standard async SMTP client. |
| I-11 | The common-password list is SecLists `Passwords/Common-Credentials/10k-most-common.txt` (MIT), vendored at `services/aurora-api/data/common-passwords.txt` and embedded with `include_str!`. | `04` requires a bundled top-10k list. |
| S-03 | The compose `aurora-sandbox` network is created with `--gateway 10.89.250.1` in addition to `--internal --disable-dns --subnet 10.89.250.0/24`. | Without an explicit gateway, podman rejects the static `10.89.250.2`/`.3` addresses `15` assigns to gateway/runner. |
| I-12 | Policy rule JSON (`policy_rules`, `derived_rules.rule`, policy API bodies): targets are tagged with `"type"` (`app`, `connection`, `app_connections`, `secret`, `secrets`, `network`); identities keep `"kind"` (`agent`, `user`). Invalid stored patterns deserialize but match nothing (fail closed). | `07` names the target kinds but no tag field, and `secrets` targets already have a `kind` field. Defined by BE-020 (`aurora-policy::rule`). |
| I-13 | The gateway destination guard resolves names with the system resolver (`tokio::net::lookup_host`) instead of `hickory-resolver`; every returned address is checked and the gateway dials only a checked address (no re-resolution). | Same security property (SEC-06) with one dependency fewer; hickory can be swapped in behind `guard.rs` if resolver control (timeouts, DoT) is needed. |
| I-14 | Manifest YAML shapes for mechanisms `09 §5` lists but `§3.4` does not exemplify, keyed by stage under `hosts[]`: `rewrite_host: { template, zone }`, `transform: { git_commit_trailer: { trailer } }`, `intercept: { oauth_token_endpoint: { path, method } }`, `passthrough: [registry_token_challenge]` (alongside `inject: [...]`, `tenant: {field, zone}`, `finalize: { aws_sigv4: {...} }`). Pinned by `crates/aurora-registry/tests/fixtures/manifests/valid-full.yaml`. | Spec gives parameters per mechanism and its stage but no YAML for these four. |
| I-15 | A tool group `wildcard` is a "true superset" of its group when the group is non-empty, every item parses, and every item host/path lies within some `hosts[].match` (host pattern + `path_prefix`) of the app; the wildcard then denotes exactly the union of the group items. Violation code `wildcard_not_superset`. Package decompressed-size cap = 4 × `PACKAGE_MAX`. | `09 §6` requires the superset proof but gives no algorithm (REG-002). |
| I-16 | `approvals.summary` / `ApprovalSummary` JSON = `{ title, fields: [{label, value}], text|null, link|null, group|null, source: "template"|"hook"|"generic" }` (WIT `summary` record + provenance), clamped to `SUMMARY_LIMITS` and sanitized; the generic summarizer redacts JSON body values to type/length and never stores raw values. Type lives in `aurora-summary`. | `03` references an `ApprovalSummary` shape not spelled out outside the WIT record. |
| S-04 | `cli_logins.hostname text null` added; the CLI key is minted on **consume** (not approval), so no reversible token is stored between approve and consume. | `03` minted the key on approval yet returned the token on consume (would require storing it) and had nowhere to keep the hostname used for the key name (`04 §CLI login`). |
| I-17 | Governance (`aurora_llm::governance`): empty org `key_scopes`/`allowed_models` inherit the instance; an empty instance∩org intersection makes the endpoint non-permitted (fail closed); method `endpoint` compliance also requires `status=verified` and current `value_version`; when the instance endpoint has no key-origin rule an org may pin its own origin using the provider natural method (`provider_account` for org/workspace ids, `litellm_team` for team ids), else 422. | `08 §11.2` leaves these edge cases implicit; choices only tighten. |
| I-18 | Host-claim decisions apply to **connected groups** of overlapping claims (from different packages): `claim_key` = `lowercase(host)|path_prefix` (`/` when absent), group key = smallest member key, group fingerprint = sha256 over sorted `package|app|key|claim_fp` lines; a claim fingerprint covers the entry injection definition. Path overlap is conservative string-prefix. | `09 §7` defines per-claim winners but not how to key decisions for multi-way overlaps (REG-003). |
| I-19 | The `aws_sigv4` finalizer is implemented in the gateway over HMAC-SHA256 (no `aws-sigv4` crate), verified against the AWS SigV4 test suite vector `get-vanilla`. More vectors (query, headers, unsigned payload) are a follow-up in GW-011. | Avoids the AWS SDK dependency tree for one function; correctness pinned by official vectors. |
| I-20 | Hook-host calls (W9) authenticate with `Authorization: Bearer <AURORA_INTERNAL_SECRET as configured (base64 text)>`; the gateway reads the hook host address from `gateway.hookhost_url`. Templated OAuth token endpoints are re-checked at runtime: rendered URL must be https on a host matching the declared zone, else `credential_unavailable` (SEC-17). | W9 says "internal secret" without the encoding; GW-011 review. |
| I-02a | (2026-11-06) The owner installed the distro `rust-wasm` package: `wasm32-wasip2` is available to the system toolchain; a probe cdylib compiles to a WebAssembly component. I-02 blocker resolved. | — |
| I-21 | Workspace MSRV is **1.88** (code uses let-chains; was mis-declared 1.85). `lru` ≥ 0.18 and `time` ≥ 0.3.47 for RUSTSEC-2026-0253/0009. `deny.toml` ignores, with reasons: RUSTSEC-2023-0071 (rsa via openidconnect — verification only), RUSTSEC-2024-0436 (paste, build-time via utoipa-axum), RUSTSEC-2024-0449 (unsafe-libyaml via spec-pinned serde_yaml). | 2026-11-06 full-workspace audit (409 tests, clippy, fmt, guards, deny all green after this). |
| I-22 | `hooks.wasm` components may import, besides `aurora:plugin/host@1.0.0` (+ `types`), the WASI 0.2 interfaces Rust std always links: `wasi:cli/*`, `wasi:io/{error,poll,streams}`, `wasi:clocks/*`, `wasi:random/random`. The hook host links these with **empty sandboxes** (no env vars, no args, no preopens, stdin closed, stdout/stderr to the capped redacted log); `wasi:filesystem`, `wasi:sockets` and anything else are rejected by the validator. `permissions.hooks` vs exports is checked by world shape only (implemented vs default hooks are indistinguishable). | `09 §2/§8.1` says "no WASI imports used", which wasm32-wasip2 std cannot satisfy; equivalent isolation is kept by the host (HOOK-001). |
| S-05 | `plugin_packages.violations jsonb not null default '[]'` added (migration `…0013`) to store validator violations for `invalid` rows (contract C-16). | `03` had no place for the violation list the admin UI must show. |
| S-06 | W6 `file.part` carries ≤ 144 KiB decoded (was 192 KiB). | 192 KiB decoded ⇒ 256 KiB base64 + envelope > `WS_FRAME_MAX` (PROTO-001). |
| S-07 | Reports the runner derives from supervisor frames use `report_id = UUIDv5(NAMESPACE_AURORA_REPORT, "<boot_id>:<seq>")` (namespace constant in `aurora-protocol`). | Runner-generated random ids made replays after a runner restart non-idempotent (PROTO-001). |
| S-08 | W5 `/work` response carries `reconciler: bool` (true only for the oldest live replica of the runner, computed by the API at poll time); runners reconcile only after a response with `true`; absent ⇒ false (fail safe). | `11` says "the oldest live replica performs reconciliation" but `16` gave replicas no way to know it (RUN-001). |
| I-23 | The supervisor cannot remove `AURORA_BOOT` from its own environment without `unsafe` (`std::env::remove_var` is unsafe in edition 2024; workspace forbids unsafe). Equivalent protection: the supervisor sets `PR_SET_DUMPABLE=0` first thing (so same-uid processes cannot read `/proc/<pid>/environ` or memory) and spawns every child through an env-scrubbing builder. The SEC-34 e2e probe MUST assert an agent process cannot read the supervisor environ and that no child env contains `AURORA_BOOT`. | `11 §The boot document` "removes it from its environment" — intent (not agent-visible) preserved (SUP-001). |
| S-09 | MCP bridge ⇄ supervisor (`/run/aurora/tools.sock`) uses newline-delimited JSON `{id, session, method: "list" | "call", tool?, arguments?}` → `{id, result}` / `{id, error:{code,message}}` with `aurora-protocol::tools` types. | `12 §Path` named the socket but not the format (PROTO-001). **Note (FIX-151, D-301):** the wire format is unchanged; `session` is a cross-check, the authority is the caller's process ancestry, and a mismatch is answered `{error: {code: "session_not_caller"}}`. |
| I-24 | `profile.dev` sets `incremental = false`: incremental caches reached 44 GB and filled the disk (2026-11-06). Prune stale `target/debug/deps` with `cargo clean` when no build is running. Refined by I-36 (`dev-fast` profile for the developer loop only). | I-03 disk constraint. |
| I-25 | Runner report idempotency (`01` "idempotency in the database") is guaranteed by **idempotent handlers**: each report kind is applied with boot/owner-fenced conditional updates or unique keys (e.g. transcript events unique per (turn, n)), so a replay on any API replica is a no-op. An in-memory recent-`report_id` set per replica is only an optimization. Every report-handling task must include a replay-on-a-second-replica test. | No report ledger table exists in `03`; handler idempotency avoids one (BE-050). |
| S-10 | `sandboxes.start_failures int not null default 0` (migration `…0014`): consecutive failed starts for the start backoff; reset when running. | `11 §Lifecycle` requires start backoff after failures but `03` had no counter (BE-050). |
| S-11 | Inbound attachment delivery (16 §W5 says only "runner downloads"): new W6 runner→supervisor kind **`file.in`** `{ turn_id, attachment_id, filename, index, last, size, sha256, data_b64 }` (≤ 144 KiB decoded per frame, same bound as S-06). For each attachment of a `turn.deliver`, the runner fetches `GET /runner/attachments/{id}` and sends all its `file.in` frames **before** forwarding `turn.deliver` (channel order is seq order). The supervisor writes `/workspace/.aurora/inbox/<turn_id>/<sanitized filename>` (temp file + rename after sha256/size match; `[A-Za-z0-9._-]`, ≤ 128; no path components) and, if a listed attachment is missing or corrupt at `turn.deliver`, still runs the turn with a `turn.notice`-style line in the input naming the missing file. `send_file` is a **supervisor-local** tool (like `process_*`) listed in `tools/list`; it refuses paths under `/workspace/.aurora/` (owner, 2026-11-06; `12 §Outbound files`). | 16 has no runner→supervisor file frame; the runner cannot write into a PVC/volume it does not mount. |
| S-12 | Approval codes for `approve <code>` / `deny <code>` (13 §4.5 fallback): RFC 4648 base32 (upper case) of the first 40 bits of **`sha256(approval id)`**, 8 characters, one implementation (`channels::cards::approval_code`). Matching scans the clicker's pending approvals in that space; an ambiguous code is refused and pointed to the dashboard. | 13 says "first 8 base32 chars of the approval id"; ids are UUIDv7, whose leading 48 bits are a millisecond timestamp, so codes of approvals created within minutes collide. |
| S-13 | Memory content is bounded **twice**: `MEMORY_CONTENT_MAX` (100 000 characters, 10 §Memory) **and** `MEMORY_CONTENT_JSON_MAX` (180 KiB once serialized as a JSON string), checked on every write path (dashboard, API, harvest). A violating write is refused (400 on `/content`). The home-sync packer can then always place a memory entry in one `HOME_SYNC_PART_MAX` part; it logs an error if an oversized entry ever appears. | TEST-014: 100 000 four-byte characters exceed a 200 KiB part, and the packer silently skipped such entries, so the agent lost the memory. |
| S-14 | `oauth2` connect methods may declare optional `fields` (09 §3.4), restricted to `store: metadata \| tenant_host`; credentials come from the token response only (violation `oauth_field_store`). `oauth_clients[].authorize_url` may be a `{template, zone}` like `token_url`. A first connect takes `connection.tenant_host` from these fields, validated against the app's tenant zone (DNS name, no IP literal/userinfo/port) before the authorize redirect, the token exchange or any probe; the host is sealed in the OAuth state. | 09 required tenant-templated token URLs but oauth2 methods had no way to collect the tenant host on first connect (FIX-006 / FUP-M3). |
| S-15 | Mattermost connector (MM-001, phase 1): (1) Mattermost (11.7, the supported version, and 12.0.2) does not return `email_verified` for users other than the caller (even to a system admin), so the connector reports `email_verified` only when the API states it and otherwise `None`; verified-email auto-linking (RFE-001, opt-in) therefore never triggers on such servers and the sign-in link flow is used. (2) `thread_ref` is the post's `root_id`, or none for a top-level post (the core roots a new thread on it). (3) The slash-command token is stored with the integration credentials (`POST /v1/connector/credentials`), since connectors cannot write integration config. (4) `link` from the slash command is forwarded as `command.invoked`; the core sends the sign-in link. | `20` assumed `GET /users/{id}` exposes `email_verified` and `thread_ref = root_id \|\| post id`; verified against Mattermost 12.0.2 in MM-001 and re-verified on 11.7.15 in MM-003 (findings 1-4 hold unchanged; slash-command and interactive-action payloads are the same). |
| I-26 | `aurora-channels::render_card` fallback: without buttons/menus, the card renders a dashboard link plus `approve <code>`/`deny <code>` commands when the platform has `commands` (13 §4.5); for non-approval cards, options render as a numbered text list (display choice, not a spec requirement). `schemas/channels_*.json` are owned by aurora-channels. | CHAN-001; brief example went beyond §4.5. |
| I-27 | `04 §Permission matrix` rows gate **reads and writes** of the listed resources alike: org policy (incl. generations/warnings), org secrets/connections/OAuth clients, app availability → org owner/admin; workspace policy (incl. generations/warnings), secrets, connections, OAuth clients, access bindings, agent grants, workspace audit → `can_own`. **Exception (grant candidates, 07 §Grants "org-scoped connections and secrets can be granted to agents in any workspace of the org")**: `GET /v1/orgs/{org}/secrets`, `GET /v1/orgs/{org}/connections` and `GET /v1/secrets/{id}` / `GET /v1/connections/{id}` of org scope (metadata only, values never returned) are also readable by an org member who is `can_own` on at least one workspace of that org; writes stay owner/admin. Workspace members (`can_use`) see what they need through `GET /v1/agents/{agent}/effective`, `GET /v1/apps?workspace_id=` and activity. `GET /v1/governance/instance` stays readable by any signed-in user (05: S; config without secrets; shown read-only on org governance pages). Resources a caller cannot see answer 404 per `05 §Request scoping` (403 only where the parent is visible). | TEST-010 found member-level reads; 05 requires every handler to run the matrix check and the matrix does not distinguish verbs. |
| I-28 | Connector manifests (BE-070): the signature is the detached SIGNATURE text over `sha256(canonical JSON of the typed manifest)` (same canonicalization as snapshots), verified with the plugin trust store (`aurora_registry::signature::verify_digest`). `package.id` must equal the provisioned connector id (422 `connector_id_mismatch`). The last approved manifest and the widening reasons are kept as private envelope keys `_approved` / `_widening` inside `connectors.manifest` (no schema column; stripped by `manifest::public` before any output). Ownership: lease 45 s, live replica = seen within 30 s, quota `ceil(active integrations / live replicas)`, surplus released at the next config poll. | 03 has `approved_manifest_digest` but no column for the approved content needed by the widening diff (13 §3). |
| I-29 | `request_app_connection {app_id, reason}` (12 catalogue; behaviour unspecified): the API validates that `app_id` is in the active catalogue and available to the agent's org (app availability, C-20), then appends a durable `notice` to the calling conversation with `code: "app_connection_requested"`, the app id/name, the sanitized one-line reason (≤ 300 chars, labelled as the agent's words) and a dashboard deep link to the app's connect window for the agent's workspace. It returns `{ "status": "requested" }`. No new table; a channel card (`channel_cards.subject_kind = connection_request`) is added with M6 mirroring. Unknown/unavailable app → tool error naming `list_apps`. | 12 lists the tool but no doc defines its effect; a conversation notice reaches the person who can act without new state. |
| I-30 | "Live" LLM acceptance (M5 `claude-code` conformance, hosted e2e with the real CLI) runs against the **real LiteLLM proxy** (pinned image, compose profile `llm-mock`) whose model list points at **VidaiMock** (`github.com/vidaiUK/VidaiMock`, Apache-2.0, Rust; image `ghcr.io/vidaiuk/vidaimock` pinned **by digest**, v0.4.0 line). VidaiMock provides Anthropic `/v1/messages` (JSON + SSE in the Anthropic event order), `count_tokens`, deterministic error injection (`X-Mock-Status`, `status_code` templates, scenario `.status` files) and per-event delays. Its bundled Anthropic template is too thin for Claude Code (tool input always `{}`, single content block, no further tool calls once any `tool_result` is in history), so Aurora ships its **own provider config, Tera templates and scenario files** under `deploy/compose/vidaimock/` (scenarios selected by `scn-<id>` model names mapped in LiteLLM's model list, or by rules on the request). No fork, no code changes to VidaiMock. A first-party mock is built **only** if a concrete conformance case cannot be expressed this way (report which). A run against a real model stays an optional manual release check. | Owner (2026-11-06): no real LiteLLM endpoint; use an existing mock (VidaiMock) behind compose rather than building one. |
| I-31 | Avatar image handling (BE-067) uses the `image` crate with `default-features = false` and only the `png`, `jpeg` and `webp` features (decoders for the three accepted types, PNG encoder for the output). Decoding runs under `image::Limits` (4096×4096, 64 MiB allocation) inside `spawn_blocking`; the type is sniffed from the bytes; output is always a freshly encoded 256×256 RGBA PNG, so metadata and trailing bytes cannot survive (SEC-30). `cargo deny check` stays green (all new transitive crates are MIT/Apache/Zlib/BSD). | Re-encoding is the only safe way to serve user-supplied images publicly (no polyglots, no EXIF/GPS); explicit limits refuse decompression bombs. |
| I-32 | Home-sync generation bumps (12 §Home projection: any change to skills, memory, brief, name, presences, peers, grants/connected apps, memory language, timezone or tool availability) are maintained by **database triggers** (migration `20261107000002_home_sync_bumps`), as an exception to the repository-layer rule of 03 (which governs `updated_at`). Open SSE streams count as session activity (they re-check the session every 10 s and touch it), so an open dashboard tab does not idle out before `SESSION_ABSOLUTE`. | FIX-005 found six change sources whose bumps had been forgotten in application code; triggers make a missed bump impossible. |
| I-33 | `app_connections.requested_scopes text[] not null default '{}'` (migration `20261107000020`): scopes requested at authorize time. `Connection.missing_scopes` = requested − granted (C-42); old rows have `{}` so it is `[]`. | C-14 `missing_scopes` was only in the connect response; the connection and callback page need it too (FIX-006). |
| I-34 | **One integration-test binary per crate** (OPS-016): `tests/it/main.rs` with a `mod` per file and shared helpers in `tests/it/common/`; ids are `file::name`. Kept separate: `aurora-api/tests/sec10_logs.rs` (scoped tracing subscriber must not share a process), `aurora-supervisor/tests/conformance.rs` (live-gated), the e2e crates, single-file crates. New test files are added as `mod` lines, not as `tests/*.rs`. Measured: 96 → 22 integration binaries, clean test build 625 → 491 s, `target/` 12 → 4.5 GB (full test run 20 → 5.9 GB), 896 tests before and after. | Keeping one binary per file (the compile and the hundreds-of-MB output are paid 96 times). Merging `sec10_logs` (failed: "the capture must be live"). |
| I-35 | **Lanes clean up after themselves** (OPS-016): the coordinator runs `scripts/dev/clean-lane.sh <worktree>` (kills processes whose cwd is in the worktree, removes podman objects named with the lane prefix or labelled `aurora.lane=<basename>`, removes `target/`) before `git worktree remove`; `scripts/dev/disk-report.sh` reports sizes; `just clean-stale` uses cargo-sweep if present. Lanes must not set `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` and must prefix their podman objects. The shared `aurora-dev-pg` is never touched. | Workers deleting their own target on `worker_done` (the coordinator may still need to rebuild or inspect it). Pattern-matching on `aurora-*` (would hit other lanes). |
| I-36 | **`dev-fast` profile** (refines I-24): `[profile.dev-fast]` inherits `dev` with `incremental = true`, used only by `just dev` / `migrate` / `build-fast` (override `AURORA_DEV_PROFILE=dev`). `dev`, `test`, CI and lanes keep `incremental = false`. Measured: one-line change in `aurora-api` 7–14 s vs 127–243 s; 3.0 GB for the service graph. I-24's 44 GB came from per-file test binaries in every lane's target, which I-34/I-35 remove, and incremental never applies to registry dependencies. | Incremental in `dev` (would bring back the 44 GB in lanes and CI). Nothing for the developer loop. |
| I-37 | **Linker**: `.cargo/config.toml` uses `scripts/dev/linker.sh` (clang + lld when installed, else `cc`; `AURORA_LD=mold\|system`); `.cargo` is excluded from image builds. Measured gain at the current debuginfo level: not distinguishable from noise (largest link 0.77 s lld, 0.77–0.91 s system), kept as zero-cost and fallback-safe. | Hard `linker = "clang"` + `-fuse-ld=lld` (breaks `rust:1-bookworm` and CI runners without lld). |
| I-38 | **cargo-nextest is optional, not the default**: `just test` = `cargo test`; `just test-nextest` and `.config/nextest.toml` (retries 0, 60 s slow, 10 min kill, 8 threads) exist. Process-per-test with 16 threads exhausted the shared Postgres (`too many clients`, 2 failures); at 8 threads no win was measurable. Revisit with a higher `max_connections`. | Making nextest the default (needs an owner install and failed under the shared DB). |
| I-39 | **sccache is optional** (`RUSTC_WRAPPER=sccache`, `SCCACHE_CACHE_SIZE=20G`, cache in `~/.cache/sccache`), never set in the repo config so nothing breaks without it. Measured: warm-cache clean test build 244 s vs 491 s uncached (cold cache 546 s), 616 MB cache; cross-worktree hits not measured (workspace-crate paths differ). Details in `docs/ops/build-performance.md` §4. | Setting `build.rustc-wrapper` in `.cargo/config.toml` (fails where sccache is absent). |
| I-40 | **Dashboard fonts are self-hosted from pinned npm packages** (P2-UI): Inter, Space Grotesk and JetBrains Mono come from `@fontsource-variable/inter`, `@fontsource-variable/space-grotesk` and `@fontsource-variable/jetbrains-mono` (exact versions in `web/package.json`, OFL-1.1). They are loaded with `next/font/local`, which gives preload and a size-adjusted fallback, and are served from `/_next/static`. Not `next/font/google`, which downloads at build time (breaks offline/air-gapped image builds), and not a font CDN, which the CSP (`default-src 'self'`) blocks. The CSP stays unchanged. | Same-origin assets keep the strict CSP; the lockfile pins the bytes, so builds are reproducible and need no network beyond the npm registry. |
| I-41 | **Theme preference is per browser and applied before first paint** (P2-UI): the choice (`system` \| `light` \| `dark`, default `system`) lives in `localStorage` (`aurora.theme`) and in a `SameSite=Strict`, non-HttpOnly cookie `aurora_theme` (no secret, path `/`, 1 year). The root layout reads the cookie server-side and renders `<html data-theme=…>` for explicit Light/Dark choices. For `system` (and as a fallback), a **constant** inline script in `<head>`, carrying the request's CSP nonce, resolves `prefers-color-scheme` and sets `data-theme` before first paint, so `script-src` stays nonce-only. It is the only inline script and contains no interpolated data. The preference is never sent to the API. | A per-user server setting would need an API and schema change for a cosmetic preference. Without the cookie or script, every load of a dark-theme page would flash light first; the cookie alone cannot follow a live system change, and the script alone flashes on a slow first paint. |
| I-42 | **kind + Helm replaces podman compose as the only containerized dev/test setup** (OPS-017, phase 1; owner, 2026-11-08: "maintain just the Kubernetes setup"). `deploy/compose/`, the `just compose-*` recipes, `docs/operator/install-compose.md` and the Caddyfile check are removed; `just kind-up / kind-dev / kind-status / kind-down` (`deploy/dev/kind-dev.sh`) drive a cluster `aurora-dev` with ingress-nginx publishing the single URL `http://localhost:4000` and Helm values profiles `deploy/kind/values-{dev,llm-mock,connector-fake}.yaml`. LiteLLM + VidaiMock (I-30) move to `deploy/dev/llm-mock/` (a Kubernetes manifest for the dev cluster; `scripts/e2e/llm.sh` keeps running its own containers from the same config and pinned digests). The chart gains `networkPolicy.gatewayExtraEgress` (extra gateway egress rules) so a private LLM endpoint can be opened narrowly next to `gateway.allowed_private`. Host-process `just dev` and the podman-based e2e harnesses (`hosted.sh`, `gateway.sh`, `llm.sh`, `channels.sh`, `mattermost.sh`, `upgrade.sh`) are unchanged; `m1.sh` defaults to the host-process stack and takes `--kind`. Service images for the dev loop are built by a builder container with a persistent cargo target volume and copied into thin distroless images (host binaries are not usable: the host glibc is newer than the distroless base). The single-host compose install is not replaced for operators: single-host installs use the Helm chart (k3s or similar). | Compose duplicated the chart (a second routing config, secrets setup, CA minting, a hosted profile that needed a different runner backend and could not run podman sandboxes from a container) and could not exercise NetworkPolicy, the Kubernetes runner backend or the Ingress path. Measurements: `docs/ops/dev-environments.md`. Supersedes the compose parts of D-31, S-03 and I-30 (compose profile `llm-mock`). |
| I-43 | **Live reload in development is watch → incremental rebuild → restart, with a stdlib-only watcher** (OPS-018, phase 1). `just dev` runs `scripts/dev/live.py`: it takes each service's workspace-crate closure from `cargo metadata`, polls source mtimes (150 ms), runs one `cargo build` for only the affected binaries while the old process keeps serving, then SIGTERM-restarts them (SIGKILL after 0.5 s), multiplexing logs with a per-service prefix and printing the measured save→listening time. Ctrl-C stops every process group. `next dev` supplies Fast Refresh; the existing rewrite + CSP already allow HMR. Real hot patching (subsecond/dioxus-style) is not used: it targets UI frameworks, not tokio servers with global state. Socket handover (`systemfd`/`listenfd`) is not adopted: it needs listener-inheritance code in four services for a window of well under a second (measured in `docs/ops/live-reload.md`), and the Next.js proxy retries nothing. No tool needs installing. `just kind-live` re-runs `kind-dev` for the affected services on each save (host-built binaries cannot run in the distroless images, I-42); Tilt `live_update` was evaluated and not adopted. **Dev-only supervisor mount**: `runner.dev_supervisor_binary` (absolute path) is mounted read-only over `/usr/local/bin/aurora-supervisor` in new podman sandboxes. `aurora-config` refuses it unless the backend is `podman` and `server.bind`, `server.external_url` and `runner.api_url` are loopback, and the path has no `:`, `,`, `..` or control characters; the runner warns on every start and fails if the file is missing. Kubernetes is not supported. `deploy/dev/env.sh` now also exports `AURORA_BOOTSTRAP_RUNNER_TOKEN` so the host runner registers. | `watchexec`/`bacon`/`cargo-watch` (an install for something 200 lines of Python do, and none gives per-service affected-set builds); Tilt for kind (an install, and its sync of a host binary is unusable with I-42); `systemfd` socket handover; a mount flag honoured on any bind address | The owner's loop stays zero-install and measurable; the guard makes a host-path mount into a sandbox impossible on anything but a developer's loopback instance. |
| I-44 | **Image builds compile through sccache, and the kind e2e uses the dev build path** (OPS-023, phase 1). (a) `images/services/Containerfile` installs a pinned, checksum-verified sccache in the `chef` stage and runs the cargo-chef `cook` and the release `build` through `with-sccache` (`RUSTC_WRAPPER=sccache`). Cache backend: a `--mount=type=cache` directory locally. (In CI the GitHub Actions cache backend was tried, with the runtime URL/token as BuildKit secrets, and **removed on 2026-11-08 after measuring**: 0 hits on consecutive runs and `images-builder` doubled to ~32 min, because its entries and the BuildKit layer cache evicted each other in the 10 GB Actions cache; `docs/ops/build-performance.md` §CI measurement.) Without a backend (fork PR, plain `podman build`) sccache falls back to its local directory, or to no sccache. Release images are unchanged (profile, distroless, non-root, SBOM, provenance). (b) `scripts/e2e/kind.sh` builds the four Rust service images with the OPS-017 builder container + thin images by default (sourcing `deploy/dev/kind-dev.sh` in a subshell), debug profile, `--release-images` (or `AURORA_E2E_RELEASE_IMAGES=1`) restores the Containerfile build. CI does not run kind.sh and keeps the release build; dev images are local-only and never pushed. | sccache `cargo install`ed in the image; secrets as build args; host-built binaries for kind (glibc, see I-42); a separate dev builder for the e2e | Any source edit invalidates `COPY . .`, so layer caching alone recompiles every workspace crate; sccache reuses unchanged crates. Secrets keep the runtime token out of layers, the cache export and provenance. The e2e proves behaviour, not the release profile, so it can use the 30-40 s dev path. |
| I-45 | **Incremental compilation for every local dev/test loop** (VEL-004, owner 2026-11-13; refines I-24/I-36; the brief called it I-37, which I-37 (linker) already occupies). `[profile.dev] incremental = true` (test inherits it); `dev-fast` stays only as an alias (`inherits = "dev"`) so `just dev`, `build-fast` and `AURORA_DEV_PROFILE` keep working. CI stays non-incremental through `CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0` in the workflow env of `ci.yml` and `nightly.yml` (throw-away targets; sccache cannot cache incremental crates). Only workspace members are incremental, so registry dependencies stay sccache-cached; `scripts/dev/rustc-wrapper.sh` passes incremental rustc calls straight through and unsets `CARGO_INCREMENTAL` for sccache (sccache aborts every call when it is set). Disk is bounded by I-34 (one test binary per crate), I-35 (per-lane targets deleted after the lane) and `just clean-stale` (prunes `target/*/incremental/<crate>-<hash>` dirs untouched for N days). Do **not** export `CARGO_INCREMENTAL=1` — the profile already does it for workspace crates only; the env var would also make registry deps incremental and uncacheable. Measurements: `docs/ops/build-performance.md` §6. | Keeping `incremental = false` (132–250 s per api edit→test cycle); a separate `[profile.test]`; requiring cargo-sweep | Waiting on rebuilds is the largest remaining cost of every lane; the 44 GB I-24 feared came from per-file test binaries that I-34/I-35 removed. |

## OPS-014: gateway performance benchmarks

- No benchmark crate was approved in `docs/02`, and none was added. `services/aurora-gateway/benches/gateway_perf.rs`
  is a `harness = false` bench built on the dependencies the gateway already has (hyper client, tokio);
  `scripts/perf/gateway.sh` drives it and reports PASS/FAIL against docs/18 §Performance budgets.
- (a) and (c) measure the real proxy + policy pipeline in process against an in-memory policy source
  (no database, so store latency is excluded as the budget says) and a local mock upstream; (a) subtracts the
  direct-to-upstream latency measured interleaved on the same connection type. (b) runs the same pipeline as a
  separate process pinned to one CPU with `taskset`, with the upstream and the load generator on other CPUs.
  The production binary needs Postgres and was not used for (b).

## Design-review decisions (owner, 2026-11-08; `80`)

Owner verdicts on `docs/80-design-review.md`, one row per item scheduled into
phases 1, 2 and 3 (D-59 is RFE-025, phase 2; D-60…D-67 are the phase-3 items). The owning docs carry the normative text, tagged
"(phase N, RFE-nnn)".

| Id | Decision | Alternatives rejected | Rationale |
|----|----------|----------------------|-----------|
| D-43 | **RFE-001 (phase 1)**: connector-asserted verified-email auto-linking is an explicit per-integration, org-admin opt-in (`config.verified_email_autolink`, default off, requires the `email_verified` capability, audited). When off, the sign-in-link flow applies and the platform email only pre-fills it. `13 §5.1/§5.9`, SEC-19/20. | Always on (phase 0); one-time dashboard confirmation only | A compromised connector must not be able to mint a link to an admin and approve as them; operators who fully trust their connector can still opt in. |
| D-44 | **RFE-002 (phase 1)**: the gateway proxy listener (4102) is **TLS-only**, used by sandboxes and external BYO agents alike; no plaintext listener. Default server certificate = a leaf issued by the existing gateway CA for the advertised host(s) and in-cluster names (everything already trusts `ca.pem`); an operator-supplied certificate is optional. `HTTPS_PROXY=https://…` in container configs; the supervisor loopback forwarder connects over TLS. The client→forwarder hop on sandbox loopback stays plain HTTP (credential-free, never leaves the sandbox). `06 §Proxy listener TLS`, SEC-40. | Keep a plaintext in-cluster listener (RFE-002 option 1); "unsupported without TLS or VPN" | Credential hygiene is the platform's promise; one listener means one code path and no way to configure the weaker one. |
| D-45 | **RFE-003 (phase 1)**: (a) the open lane has a **posture** `enabled` \| `allowlist_only` \| `disabled` at instance (`governance.egress.open_lane_minimum`), org (`governance.egress.open_lane`) and workspace (policy `open_lane_posture`), tighten-only, strictest wins; (b) the open lane is evaluated at CONNECT time with explicit semantics: restrictive rules treat unknowable parts (path, method, body, tool) as matched, allow rules and allow-list entries need a full match on what is knowable, credential targets never match; (c) the supervisor opens the loopback open proxy only when the boot document carries the `browser` capability, which comes from the sandbox image label `org.aurora.capabilities` (reported by the runner) ∩ the effective posture, and the gateway independently admits the open lane only for boot credentials minted with that capability (BYO and context credentials never). `06 §Open-lane admission and posture`, `07 §CONNECT-time evaluation`, SEC-41. | Per-agent switch (no decided source); leaving the lane unconditional | Closes the uncredentialed tunnel that every sandbox process and every BYO client could open; keeps browsers working where wanted. |
| D-46 | **RFE-004 (phase 1)**: **all interactive sign-in is OIDC**; password sign-up/sign-in/verification/reset are removed (phase-tagged in `04`, `05`, `14`); the IdP enforces MFA. `bootstrap-admin` pre-provisions an instance admin who binds the identity at the first OIDC sign-in; `auth.instance_admins` works through OIDC identities; API keys and CLI device login stay; `signup_mode` is evaluated at the first OIDC sign-in (JIT); `require_email_verified` is no longer configurable (always true); account deletion needs a fresh OIDC re-authentication. Development and e2e use the packaged mock IdP (`aurora-dev-idp`, profile `dev-idp`) and `aurora_testkit::oidc`. `04 §Authentication`, SEC-42. | TOTP/WebAuthn in Aurora; OIDC-only for admins; MFA setting per org | One strong, externally-managed factor beats a second, weaker implementation; removes the phishable local password everywhere. |
| D-47 | **RFE-009 (phase 1)**: `direct` conversations are visible only to their user (while they can use the workspace) and to workspace owners (`can_own`); only the user can write; everything that names them follows (approval cards/feeds, activity rows, references, SSE, search). Owner reads are audited. `04 §The access law`, `05 §Conversation visibility`, `10`, SEC-43. **Clarification C**: visibility applies within the conversation's workspace; a cross-workspace approval (other side of `message_agent` from a DM) is decided by its own workspace but sees only the summary, with the conversation reference redacted (`07 §Action approvals` 5). | Keep them open but say so loudly; per-workspace setting | A DM the sender believes private must not be workspace-public; owners keep repair access. |
| D-48 | **RFE-016 (phase 1)**: streaming deltas flow through a `DeltaBus` trait (`publish`/`subscribe`, at-most-once, lossy allowed, coalescing above the trait); phase 1 implementation is the existing NOTIFY channel (`events.delta_bus = notify`); `15 §Sizing` states a planning ceiling (≈100 comfortable / ≈200–300 maximum concurrently streaming turns). Control signals stay on NOTIFY (D-03). `05 §The delta bus seam`. | Redis now; no seam | No behaviour change today; a later fan-out implementation becomes a registry entry. |
| D-49 | **RFE-005 (phase 2)**: policy `user` identities bind to the **turn's speaker** (hosted context credential carrying `speaker_user_id`); without a speaker (boot/agent-token/BYO-context traffic, non-person turns) `user` identities **never match** (fail closed); the workspace-roster semantics are gone. Simulate/effective take a speaker; policy warnings flag user-scoped rules. `07 §Rule anatomy`, `06 §Credential authentication`, SEC-44. | Rename the kind to "workspace member …" and add `speaker` later; roster-match fallback for speakerless traffic | A rule that reads "when Alice asks" must mean that; speakerless traffic must not inherit a person's privileges. |
| D-50 | **RFE-006 (phase 2)**: **freeze** at agent, workspace and organization level — `frozen_*` state columns, `POST …/freeze|unfreeze` routes, effects (gateway refuses credentials and closes tunnels, turns refused/aborted, due-work skips, webhooks 410, schedules skip without disabling, presences reply "paused", sandbox parked with state kept), audited atomically and reversible. Authority: agent and workspace → `can_own` (org owners/admins included); organization → org owner or instance admin; lifting needs at least the freezing authority (`frozen_authority`). Credentials are not revoked, so unfreezing is instant. `04 §Freeze`, SEC-45. | Revoke credentials on freeze; delete-only; member-level freeze | The person closest to the agent must be able to stop it fast, a higher authority's freeze must not be reversible by a lower one, and reversibility keeps forensics and avoids token redistribution. |
| D-51 | **RFE-007 (phase 2)**: `AURORA_SIGNING_KEY` and `AURORA_INTERNAL_SECRET` become **keyring-style lists** (`key_id:value`, plus `*_ACTIVE_KEY`; a bare value stays valid as ring `v0`; ≤4 entries); sign/present with the active key, verify with any; derived tokens carry their key id (action tokens `v2.`); documented rolling rotation (append everywhere → activate → remove) with `aurora-api keys {generate,status}`. The runner session key and the gateway CA are **not** covered (owner chose option a only). `08 §4`, SEC-46. | CA dual-trust rotation; KMS-wrapped keyring | Makes the two high-value static secrets rotatable as a rolling config change using the shape operators already know from the encryption keyring. |
| D-52 | **RFE-008 (phase 2)**: audit actions are **classified**; the **security-critical set** (keys and credentials, grants, policy publish/rollback, plugin/connector/trusted-key/host-claim decisions, approval decisions, roles and membership, freeze, governance, channel integrations and links, erasure and reassignment — named in `04 §Audit`) is written **in the same transaction** as the change and fails the action if it cannot be written (503 `audit_unavailable`); everything else stays best-effort. `audit_log.critical` marks the rows. SEC-32. | Keep all audit best-effort; atomic for everything | Atomicity is cheap for low-volume critical actions (same database) and removes the silent-loss window; high-volume rows must never take the platform down. |
| D-53 | **RFE-010 (phase 2)**: conversation **archive** (visibility flag) and **delete** (hard cascade + harness session purge in the sandbox home, authority: direct user or `can_own` / `can_own`), **transcript event redaction** mirroring memory redaction (payload replaced in place, `turns.text` scrubbed, optional session reset, audited), **optional per-org retention** for conversations and attachments (default none; daily sweep through the same delete path; instance ceiling can only tighten) and **JSON export** (`aurora.conversation.v1`). `10 §Archive, delete, redaction, retention, export`, SEC-47. | Delete without session purge; soft delete only | Obligations (erasure, "no logs older than N days") need real deletion including the copy in the agent's home; redaction handles the pasted-secret case without destroying the conversation. |
| D-54 | **RFE-015 (phase 2, joined with RFE-010)**: per-org attachment **retention window** (bytes purged, row kept, 410 on download) and **storage quota** (default 10 GiB, instance ceiling, **422 `attachment_quota_exceeded`** on every storing path), an operator/org **storage breakdown** (`/v1/admin/storage`, `/v1/orgs/{org}/storage`), and a daily **sweep** (retention, orphan blobs, counter reconciliation, notices). The BlobStore seam stays the long-term answer. `10 §Attachments`, SEC-48. | Unbounded growth; global-only limit | A busy workspace must not be able to fill the shared database; limits are per tenant and visible. |
| D-55 | **RFE-012 (phase 2)**: a third workspace role **`approver`** (`owner` > `approver` > `member`) and a law `can_approve` (owner, approver, org owner/admin). Plain members chat and operate agents but cannot decide gateway approvals, action approvals or agent-link requests, see the approvals feed, or click approval cards; approvers decide them; owners can too. Roles are grantable by `can_own` (also through invitations and JIT); the migration leaves existing members as members (no silent privilege). No separation-of-duties rule. `04 §The access law`, SEC-28. | A workspace setting "approvals decided by members \| owners"; full RBAC | One deliberate notch that keeps the model small while separating chatting from approving. |
| D-56 | **RFE-011a (phase 2)**: (1) **stall exemption** — a turn with a `pending` approval is not failed `turn_stalled`; the stall clock restarts at resolution (`approvals.resolved_at`); `TURN_CEILING` still applies; (2) **per-rule / per-ask-grant `approval_timeout_secs`** bounded by an instance maximum (default 3 600 s, always below the turn ceiling), effective value = minimum of the set values; (3) **e-mail to the approvers/owners** when an approval has no live delivery surface (no delivered card, no fresh watcher) after 20 s — sanitized title and dashboard link only, rate-limited, opt-out; (4) **SSE-driven browser notification** in the dashboard (opt-in, open tab). Park-and-resume and Web Push are RFE-011b (phase 4). `07 §Approval deadlines`, `07 §Approvals`, SEC-49. | Fixed 300 s for everything; always-on e-mail for every approval | Approvals must not die unseen, and the platform must not kill turns it is itself holding; timeouts stay modest and bounded because the gateway holds the connection (no park-and-resume yet). |
| D-57 | **RFE-017 (phase 2)**: a per-principal **token-bucket limiter** on the management API (in memory per replica, same approximation as the gateway) with a general bucket, stricter class buckets for messages, uploads, simulate, search and export, a per-org bucket and a stream concurrency cap; **429 + `retry-after`** in `05 §Conventions`. Machine planes and webhooks keep their own limits. `05 §Conventions`, SEC-50. | Per-IP only; shared Redis counters now | A leaked key or one busy member must not degrade the instance for everyone; the seam for shared counters already exists. |
| D-58 | **RFE-021 (phase 2)**: (a) `can_own` may **reassign** a schedule/webhook `creator_user_id` to an authorized active member (audited; bulk and atomic with member removal via `reassign_to`); (b) an **offboarding view** lists what removing a person would disable (org-wide for org owners/admins, per workspace for `can_own`); (c) **workspace-anchored** automations (authority = "an active owner exists"), creatable only by owners and never by agent tools. Peer tasks and reach decisions are unchanged. `04 §Fire-time authority`, SEC-51. | Auto-reassign to the removing admin; allow agents to anchor | Offboarding must not silently destroy team automation, yet authority stays explicit and audited. |
| D-59 | **RFE-025 (phase 2)**: BYO agents are governed. (a) A **BYO posture** `enabled` \| `owners_only` \| `disabled` with the governance layering (instance master switch `byo.enabled` and floor `byo.minimum_posture` → org `governance.byo.posture` → workspace `byo_posture`; strictest wins; default `owners_only`); `disabled` refuses creation and makes the gateway refuse existing `aug_`/BYO `auc_` credentials (nothing deleted); `owners_only` needs `can_own` to create a BYO agent or regenerate its token. (b) An optional **source CIDR allow-list** per BYO agent with org and instance defaults (tighten-only), checked at connect time (403 `source_not_permitted`); the client address is the TLS peer, or the PROXY protocol address from `gateway.trusted_proxies`, never HTTP headers. (c) Policy warning `rule_applies_to_all_agents`. (d) Per-agent (and org/instance default) **require context credentials** for credentialed requests (403 `context_credential_required`). `08 §11.9`, `06 §BYO admission`, SEC-52. | Posture only; CIDR from `X-Forwarded-For`; blocking (not warning) on broad rules | Closes the gap that the least-privileged role could mint an externally usable egress identity; every layer is tighten-only like governance, and attribution is optional but enforceable. |
| D-60 | **RFE-013 (phase 3)**: optional **maximum age for BYO agent tokens**, instance (`governance.agent_tokens.max_age_days`) and org, smaller wins, **default none**; applied at mint and, with a 7-day minimum grace, to existing tokens when a limit is introduced or tightened (never extended on loosening); context credentials capped by their token; expired = refused like revoked (`agent_token_expired`); `last_used_at` and expiry surfaced in the dashboard and in stale/expiring token lists. The regenerate flow is unchanged. `08 §7`, SEC-53. | Mandatory expiry; per-agent expiry; no retrofit of existing tokens | Opt-in hygiene with no behaviour change by default; existing integrations get a week to rotate rather than breaking at once. |
| D-61 | **RFE-014 (phase 3)**: the gateway **degrades instead of stopping** when Postgres is unreachable: (a) entries in the identity, ResolvedHost and plaintext caches are served **within their TTL only** (nothing extended or refreshed) and everything needing new state is refused fail closed (503 `gateway_degraded`, `approval_store_unavailable`; never 407 for an unreadable store); (b) on LISTEN loss the gateway **reconnects first and flushes after** re-subscribing (caches kept meanwhile); (c) `ready` / `degraded` / `down` in `/readyz` (degraded stays 200, `?strict=true` makes it 503), `aurora_gateway_state` and degraded-mode metrics. Outage tolerance equals the cache TTL (`gateway.resolve_cache_ttl_secs`); the revocation-lag consequence is documented. `06 §Operation during a Postgres outage`, SEC-54. | Serve stale beyond TTL with a grace knob; keep flush-then-reconnect; readyz 503 on degraded | Keeps authority bounded by the existing TTL while removing the self-inflicted cliff; readiness must not pull every gateway out when the shared database is the problem. |
| D-62 | **RFE-018 (phase 3)**: `POST /v1/conversations/{id}/messages` accepts an optional **`Idempotency-Key`** header (1–128 chars). It is stored in the existing `turns.source_event_id` as `api:<user_id>:<key>` (unique per conversation by the existing partial index; **scoped per acting user**, `api:` reserved against connector event ids); a replay returns the original result (same shape, current state, `Idempotent-Replayed: true`), a replay with different text or attachments is 422 `idempotency_key_reused`; authorization and visibility are always checked first; the key lives as long as the turn. `05 §Idempotency keys`, SEC-55. | Body field; per-conversation (not per-user) scope; a separate keys table with expiry | Reuses a proven mechanism with no schema change; per-user scoping and the namespace stop one principal from probing or colliding with another's keys. |
| D-63 | **RFE-019 (phase 3)**: the specification's defaults are deployment-neutral. The default `agents.default_brief` is a short neutral assistant brief (concise, ask before irreversible actions, say which tools were used); the Daneel persona moves to this deployment's `aurora.toml` and is shown as an example in `15`. The sandbox base image is the public official AlmaLinux image (`docker.io/library/almalinux:10`, digest-pinned), with the CERN `alma10-base` image as this deployment's **build-time** override (`SANDBOX_BASE_IMAGE`). Existing agents and workspaces are untouched; `agents.default_name` stays `Daneel`. `10 §Default agent`, `11 §Sandbox image`, `15 §Deployment-specific settings example`. | Keep the persona as default; runtime config key for the base image | A product default should not instruct an agent to be sassy or to deny being an AI, and other deployments cannot assume a CERN registry. The base image is consumed at build time, so a build argument is the honest mechanism. |
| D-64 | **RFE-020 (phase 3)**: skills gain a **workspace tier** (`skills.workspace_id`, null for agent/org skills; CHECK against agent+workspace), projected into every agent of the workspace with precedence **agent > workspace > org** and the existing shadowing rule (a disabled skill never vetoes a lower tier). Written by `can_own` only (org owners/admins included) and never by agent tools; the API computes the effective set, so the wire format is unchanged. Routes `GET/POST /v1/workspaces/{ws}/skills`, `GET …/agents/{a}/skills/effective`. `10 §Skills`, `03 §Planned changes`, SEC-56. | Workspace skills writable by `can_use`; agent tools allowed to edit them; a second workspace skills table | Matches every other shared resource (org or workspace) without a new table, keeps authority over instructions that reach all agents with owners, and avoids retrofitting shadowing semantics later. |
| D-65 | **RFE-022 (phase 3)**: on activation of a plugin **major** upgrade, in the same transaction, every connection of the package's apps whose stored credential field **names** (`app_connections.credential_keys`) no longer cover the new manifest's required fields becomes `reauth_required` with a `plugin_major_upgrade …` reason naming the version change and missing fields; the approval screen warns "N connections will need reconnecting" (`impact` on the plugin resource). Upgrades are not refused while connections exist; a major upgrade that would break connections is **not auto-activated** (needs instance-admin approval). Legacy rows without keys fall back to the old manifest, else fail closed. `09 §10`, `03 §Planned changes`, SEC-57. | Refuse the upgrade while connections exist; leave connections to fail at injection time; decrypt every credential at activation | Surfaces a schema break as a reconnect prompt instead of `credential_unavailable`, without reading secrets or blocking operators. |
| D-66 | **RFE-023 (phase 3)**: documentation, no behaviour change. The credentialed Default Rule skips governed LLM hosts so that a default-deny of tool credentials cannot brick the agent's own model loop; LLM traffic is controlled by LLM governance (`08 §11`), and **explicit rules naming an LLM host still win** (rules precede defaults). `07 §Why the Default Rule skips LLM hosts` explains it, `08 §11.4` points back, the policy editor's Default Rule control says "Does not affect LLM endpoints — see Governance", and simulation reports `default_rule_skipped_llm_host`. | Apply the Default Rule to LLM hosts; remove the carve-out | A surprising exemption in a policy engine needs a stated rationale and a visible UI note; removing it would force an allow rule per LLM endpoint for every default-deny deployment. |
| D-67 | **RFE-024 (phase 3)**: connector `presence.deprovision` operations are **self-contained** and committed **in the same transaction as the delete, before the cascade** (agent, workspace, org, detach). Each op has `integration_id = NULL` (so the cascade cannot remove it; any live connector replica may claim it), snapshots `platform_user_ref`, handle, tenant and non-secret config, and carries a **sealed copy of the needed credentials bound to the op** (opened at claim, discarded at the terminal state or pruning). Retries run up to 24 h; if deprovision still fails the orphaned bot is a documented residual with a `presence.deprovision_failed` audit row. Shared bots are not deprovisioned. `13 §4.2`, `04 §Deletion semantics`, SEC-58. | State deprovision is skipped and orphaned bots are the residual; ops that look the rows up at claim time | Makes the best-effort cleanup executable by construction without keeping the deleted rows alive; the credential copy is short-lived and op-bound. |
| D-69 | **RFE-021 implementation details (phase 2, refines D-58)**: (a) `reassign_to` travels as a query parameter on the two DELETE routes (no body on DELETE) and as a body field on the suspending PATCH; (b) schedules/webhooks keep `created_by` (`{id, display_name}`) as the creator field and add `anchor`; a separate `creator` field would only duplicate it; (c) switching an item back to `anchor = creator` keeps the stored creator as authority and requires them to still be authorized (422 `new_creator_unauthorized`); (d) bulk and `reassign_to` moves leave workspace-anchored items alone unless `ids` names them; (e) the offboarding workspace view of an org owner/admin is empty, and its `api_keys` counts only workspace-confined keys; (f) the `automation.reassign` audit row is written in the same transaction as the change (`audit::insert_tx`), failing closed with 503 `audit_unavailable`, ahead of the RFE-008 generic mechanism. `C-90..C-95`, SEC-51. | A `creator` object next to `created_by`; request bodies on DELETE | Smallest wire change that satisfies the spec; the change and its audit row cannot diverge. |
| D-71 | **RFE-025 implementation details (phase 2, refines D-59)**: (a) CIDR syntax is strict like PostgreSQL `cidr` (host bits must be zero; `10.0.0.1/24` is refused, not widened), shared by agent/org/instance lists and `gateway.trusted_proxies` (`aurora_core::cidr`, no new dependency); `/0` is refused everywhere, including trusted proxies. (b) Unparsable stored values fail closed, mirroring the open-lane posture: a workspace posture text or an org `byo` document that does not parse is `disabled`, an unparsable stored source list matches nothing. (c) The effective BYO values travel with the cached gateway identity (computed in the credential resolver from instance + org + workspace + agent rows); live BYO tunnels are tracked in a registry that re-resolves the credential by id on any invalidation of its `agent`/`workspace`/`org`/`credential` scope and closes the tunnel (and its WebSockets and held requests) when the credential is gone, the posture is `disabled`, the source address is no longer permitted, or context credentials became required; loosening never closes anything. (d) `aurora run` mints its labelled context credential when the agent requires context credentials (or with `--context`), not for every run, so runs longer than the 24 h credential ceiling keep working with a plain token. (e) `request_log.context_label` and `approvals.context_label` (nullable) carry the label of rule (d); the gateway metric is named `aurora_gateway_byo_refused_total` as specified. | Always minting a context credential in `aurora run`; separate `workspace_byo` table; closing every BYO tunnel on any org/workspace invalidation | Keeps the posture layering identical to the open-lane code, never widens a list silently, fails closed on doubt, and closes only what an admission change affects. |

## Dashboard visual design (owner, 2026-11-07)

| Id | Decision | Alternatives rejected | Rationale |
|----|----------|-----------------------|-----------|
| D-68 | **Phase 2, P2-UI**: the dashboard adopts the visual design and brand identity in `14 §Visual design`. It applies to **every** screen, existing and future, not only the screens in the mock-ups (owner, 2026-11-08): the mock-ups are the references for shared primitives and page patterns that all UI uses. It has semantic colour tokens with **light and dark themes** (System / Light / Dark switch, System by default), Space Grotesk / Inter / JetBrains Mono, and a left-sidebar shell that replaces the top header and context nav. Org and workspace are switched from one control, and the sidebar collapses to a menu dialog on narrow screens. The brand is the owner's twisted-ribbon "A"-and-star mark: one teal mark in both themes, with the star ink on light and white on dark, and mono versions for small sizes. the spaced "AURORA" wordmark (lettered in Chromatic Void Sans and shipped only as outlines) and the tagline "Build · Run · Collaborate". The owner's master logos (`aurora-logo-final-{light,dark}.svg`), the variants generated from them and the reference mock-ups are in `docs/design/ui-refresh/`. No API or behaviour changes. | Keep the unstyled phase-0 look; adopt a third-party component kit's default theme; ship light only and add dark later | Owner request (2026-11-07). Users read identifiers, decisions and pending holds faster with the new design. Doing dark mode now costs only a second set of token values plus the switch, whereas retrofitting it later means re-checking every screen. The brand board is the owner's. |
| D-70 | **P2-010 implementation choices for D-53** (no change to the owner's decision): (a) `session_purges` gains `leased_until` so live `session.purge` work items are re-sent every 30 s until the supervisor reports `session.purged`; (b) a purge waits while its conversation has a turn in flight (only possible after `reset_session`; a delete is refused while a turn is active), so a purge never kills a live turn; (c) `reset_session` also clears `harness_session_ref` (the epoch bump alone would let the next turn resume the old session); (d) `POST …/redact` answers 200 `{ redacted_seqs, session_reset }` and takes a required JSON body; (e) the export document carries `format: "aurora.conversation.v1"` and `attachments=zip` is a stored (uncompressed) zip written in-process; (f) the `retention.conversation_days_max` ceiling also caps an org window stored before the ceiling was tightened (sweep uses the smaller value); (g) `event.redacted` travels on the ephemeral delta bus. `API_CONTRACTS C-120..C-128`. | A separate purge acknowledgement table; one-shot purge delivery; refusing `reset_session` during a turn | Keeps delivery at-least-once without a new queue, never kills a running turn, and makes the "forget this conversation" promise true for the next turn. |
| D-72 | **P1-005 implementation details (phase 1, refines D-46)**: (a) the development IdP is the `aurora-dev-idp` binary of the `aurora-testkit` crate (`src/bin`), not a new crate; it takes its users from `AURORA_DEV_IDP_USERS(_JSON)`, signs in an unlisted `login_hint` only with `AURORA_DEV_IDP_ALLOW_ANY_HINT=1` (set by the dev stacks and e2e harnesses, never by the chart default); the fixed client id/secret live in `aurora-config` (`DEV_IDP_CLIENT_*`) and a test keeps the mock in step. (b) `auth.dev_idp.internal_url` (optional) separates the URL the browser uses from the one the API uses, so the kind stack serves the IdP through the Ingress at `/dev-idp` while the API reaches the Service; the API rewrites requests under the issuer prefix. (c) `reauth=1` needs a live session at `start` (401 otherwise) and binds `(session, user)` into the sealed state; the callback stamps `sessions.reauth_at` and issues no new session. (d) account deletion answers `412 reauth_required` (spec) instead of 403 `reauthentication_required`; `DELETE /v1/me` takes no body. (e) A first sign-in of a *new* user under `invite_only`/`sso_jit` accepts every pending invitation for that e-mail; existing users accept explicitly. (f) `start` forwards `login_hint`. (g) `bootstrap-admin` prints `<external>/auth/login`; in the kind dev images the binary is `/usr/local/bin/aurora-service` (`kind-dev.sh bootstrap-admin`). (h) The migration `20261110000001_oidc_only_sign_in.sql` adds `sessions.reauth_at` and nulls `users.password_hash` (irreversible for that column; erasing the unused hashes now rather than at the contract migration was approved by the owner on 2026-11-08; the column and `email_tokens` stay until the contract migration). |
| D-73 | **P2-015 implementation choices for D-54** (no change to the owner's decision): (a) the `org_storage_usage` counter is maintained by **database triggers** on `attachments` (insert, purge, delete, cascades) and the quota check locks the counter row `FOR UPDATE` in the inserting transaction, so concurrent writers of one org serialize (no overshoot at all, stricter than "one file"); a cheap unlocked pre-check runs before bytes are written; (b) a row counts while it **holds bytes** (`purged_at IS NULL AND status <> 'failed'`); a purge clears `blob_key` so a blob whose delete failed is unreferenced and falls to the orphan sweep; (c) org quota resolution: the org's own `attachment_quota_mib` or the instance default, capped by `attachments.org_quota_mib_max`; `0` = unlimited (allowed for an org only when the instance has no ceiling); validation errors `attachment_quota_invalid` / `attachment_quota_above_instance_maximum`, retention reuses `retention_invalid` / `retention_above_instance_maximum`; (d) `GET/PUT /orgs/{org}/retention` additively carries the attachment keys; (e) the daily **attachment sweep** is a second singleton job sharing the daily tick of the conversation retention sweep (the hourly pending/orphan sweep stays); (f) the 80 % / 100 % **notices** are durable `attachment.quota_notice` audit rows (level `warning`/`full`/`clear`, once per crossing, written by the sweep) plus the live `notice` level of `GET /orgs/{org}/storage`, which the dashboard renders as a banner; the e-mail channel (RFE-011a) is not wired here; (g) connector `file.fetch`: the inbox checks the declared size first and classifies a refused upload as `quota_exceeded`, then posts a platform-voice notice; (h) an org-quota refusal of runner `send_file` is **422** `attachment_quota_exceeded` (the per-conversation daily cap keeps its 429 with the same code). `API_CONTRACTS C-170..C-176`. | Application-level counter updates on each path; a separate notices table | One choke point cannot be bypassed by a new insert path, and the counter stays right through cascades. |
| D-74 | **Self-hosted app instances (phase 2, owner 2026-11-08, pulled forward because the owner runs its own GitLab)**: an app may declare `instances`, and its hosts, tools, probe, OAuth URLs and hook egress may use the whole-host placeholder `{instance}` (`09 §3.4 Instance-bound hosts`). An **app instance** is a host registered for one app by an instance admin (instance-wide) or an org owner/admin (that org), validated (DNS name only; not another package's static claim, an LLM host, `aurora.internal` or another instance; optional probe through the destination guard) and audited. Connections and BYOC OAuth clients bind to an instance; the gateway injects an instance-bound connection only on its instance's exact host (SEC-59, extends SEC-04). Registered hosts take part in `09 §7` claim conflicts.
