(d) **Who sees it** follows the data's existing readers: Activity's readers for a workspace and its agents, org owners and admins for the organization (as Data's other tabs), instance admins for Administration. | A Usage section on the agent page (breaks the five-section structure; tokens are not "Computer"); a separate Usage topic in the workspace (a tenth topic for a daily page Activity already is); a stacked chart by model (needs a categorical palette the tokens do not define; models are a table instead); a chart library (one bar chart does not justify the dependency) | Usage sits where people already look at what agents did (Activity), with a short entry on the agent itself; instance admins find it beside storage and audit under Data. |
| D-361 | **Three more harnesses: Pi, goose and OpenCode, with the same treatment as Claude Code; Claude Code stays the default (owner, 2026-11-17: "we want to add also pi, goose and opencode. they should all get the same treatment as Claude, but Claude remains the default. Add this to phase 5"; P5-HARN).** (a) New harness ids are `pi` (https://github.com/earendil-works/pi), `goose` (https://github.com/aaif-goose/goose) and `opencode` (https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode). (b) **The same treatment**: each one gets an adapter in the compile-time registry, the conformance suite (CI mocks plus env-gated live runs), the default sandbox image with a pinned, checksum-verified runtime, selection per agent and `GET /v1/harnesses`, dialect-based provider reach, instructions and skills, platform tools and `aurora-agent`, the default model, roles where the harness has a slot, per-model usage counted by the usage ledger, session resume, purge and switching, the shared provider-error classes, a web-terminal command, the dashboard and the operator docs. (c) **Claude Code stays the default**: `hosted.default_harness` stays `claude-code`. They are **not enabled by default** (owner, D-363): `hosted.enabled_harnesses` keeps its default `["claude-code"]` and the new ones are opt-in like Codex. `12 §Adapters pi, goose and opencode`, C-1010..C-1016, SEC-133. | Only one of the three; a plugin or external-adapter interface instead of built-in adapters; making one of them the default | The owner's request. A built-in adapter per harness keeps D-122's properties: the API chooses, there is no fallback and no credential in the sandbox. |
| D-362 | **P5-HARN design (coordinator, 2026-11-17; how D-361 is built).** (a) **Driving**: `pi` uses one long-lived `pi --mode rpc` per live session (so it can steer); `goose` uses one `goose run --output-format stream-json` per turn, or `goose acp` if the pinned stream-json lacks tool events or usage (Part A records which); `opencode` uses one `opencode run --format json` per turn in its private in-process mode, never a shared or background server (D-363(b): v1 CLI), so that platform-tool calls descend from the harness process (D-301). (b) **State per conversation** under `<home>/.aurora/harness/<id>/sessions/<conversation_id>/`, so `purge_session` removes one directory, exactly (none of D-153(g)'s leftover index rows). Configuration is in `<home>/.aurora/harness/<id>/config/` and is re-rendered and read back before every start. Credential stores (`auth.json`, `secrets.yaml`, keyring) are deleted or switched off. (c) **Dialects**: `pi` and `opencode` declare `anthropic_messages`, `openai_responses` and `openai_chat`; `goose` declares `anthropic_messages` and `openai_chat`, plus `openai_responses` if its pinned version sends to it. None declares Bedrock or Vertex: their SDKs sign with cloud credentials and have no skip-auth switch, so `litellm` serves those. This makes `openai_chat`-only endpoints usable by an enabled harness for the first time (D-260(a)'s "until one declares `openai_chat`"); the C-811 notice follows the declared dialects. (d) **No vendor network**: telemetry, update checks, model catalogues (pi.dev, models.dev), sharing, session-naming calls and background services are off; the live suite proves that a turn reaches only its endpoint's inference paths. (e) **Tools**: the harness's own file and shell tools stay on; built-ins with their own memory store or a vendor service are switched off; MCP tools are exposed directly, not through the harness's code mode, so on-demand platform tools (D-297) behave as they do for `claude-code`. (f) **Models**: a role is mapped only to a native slot (Pi none; goose's subagent model; OpenCode's small model and general subagent), and the dashboard's "Not used by Codex" becomes "Not used by <harness>". (g) **Versions**: one pinned version each. Part A checks `12` against the binaries and corrects it, removing anything the version lacks rather than emulating it. OpenCode is pinned on its v1.19.x line (owner, D-363). (h) **Part A amendment (2026-11-18, checked against the binaries; details in `12 §Adapters pi, goose and opencode`).** Pins: Pi 1.5.2, goose 1.59.2, OpenCode 1.19.6. `goose` is driven with `goose run --output-format stream-json` (tool events and usage are in the stream), not `goose acp`. `pi` declares `steer = true` (a `prompt` with `streamingBehavior: "steer"` is queued during a run and joins before the next model call; the adapter refuses it between turns because Pi would start a run). All three use `skills_dir = ".agents/skills"`. OpenCode runs `opencode run` in-process, keeps its global config directory read-only for the agent (otherwise it installs an npm plugin in the background), and takes per-model usage from its own database; goose cannot switch off one `models.dev` catalogue fetch per start, which the session proxy refuses. | One long-lived process for every harness (goose and OpenCode per-turn runs are their documented headless path); a shared state directory per harness with targeted deletes inside SQLite stores (editing another program's database); declaring Bedrock/Vertex with credentials in the sandbox (SEC-01); leaving each harness's code mode on (hides the platform tools' on-demand index behind a second one) | Each choice keeps P4-HAR's properties and reuses its machinery. Per-conversation directories make purge exact for three different storage formats. |
| D-363 | **Owner answers Q-HARN-1 and Q-HARN-2 (2026-11-17, P5-HARN).** (a) **Not enabled by default** (Q-HARN-1: "Correct, not enabled by default"). `hosted.enabled_harnesses` keeps its default `["claude-code"]`. Pi, goose and OpenCode ship in the default image and an instance admin enables them in Administration › Agents › Defaults, as with Codex. (b) **OpenCode is pinned on the v1.19.x line** (Q-HARN-2: "Yes, ship 1.19.x"). The adapter, its rendered config and Part A's checks follow the v1 config schema and CLI (`/v1/docs`), not the v2 docs that opencode.ai now shows by default. Moving to v2 later is a version bump with its own spec change: the config schema, permission format and server API differ. | All five harnesses enabled on new installs; pinning OpenCode v2.3.0 (newer, but GitHub does not mark it Latest, and its server API is labelled experimental) | The owner's answers. |
| D-364 | **Owner, 2026-11-17: the P4-UTOK reading of "ends the token" stands ("keep it").** Removing an agent's grant, or deleting the agent or its workspace, ends *that agent's standing* with the person's token (its pending approvals rejected `credential_revoked`, its unclaimed sends failed, one `user_token.ungrant` audit row) and leaves the token for the person's other agents; the owner's delete, link removal, SCIM deprovisioning, the owner's or the integration's deletion and a platform `401` end the token itself (D-284(a) read this way). (SSH and the web terminal: D-358.) | Ending the whole token on any agent's grant removal | The person granted the token to each agent separately: one agent losing access should not cut the others off; the owner still ends the token in one step. |
| D-365 | **Workspace members may read their workspace's published Internet access rules (owner, 2026-11-17; amends I-27 for this case; FIX-164).** A member of a workspace reads that workspace's **published** Internet access policy (rules and defaults of the active generation) through a member-readable route, read-only. Drafts, generation history, warnings, other workspaces' overrides and every write stay owner-only as I-27 says. | Members see only the limits from above (I-27 unchanged) | P5-IA-2's spec has members see the workspace's Internet access read-only; knowing what their agents may reach is the point of the page; the published rules are what already applies to them. |
| D-366 | **`aurora-addons-cern` releases are numbered on their own (owner, 2026-11-17; amends D-252(b)).** Each package keeps its own version (`package.version`). A release of the repository is tagged `v1`, `v2`, `v3`, …: the next number after the highest `vN` tag on origin. It carries every package version recorded since the previous release. `scripts/release.sh record` writes that number into each **new** record. Until the tag is pushed, every new record names the same pending release. A record that exists keeps its tag, so `v0.1.0` … `v0.1.3` stay as published, and their manifest URLs do not change. `TAG=vN` still overrides the number, but it must be `v<number>` and not already on origin. CI is unchanged: `vN` matches its `v*` trigger, and the publish job takes the archives whose records name the pushed tag. `scripts/release.sh next-tag` prints the pending number. | Keep `v<package version>` (owner: "we're going to be skipping versions all the time": cern-phonebook skipped 0.1.1 and cern-maps 0.1.2 because the other package held the tag); one tag per package (`cern-maps-v0.1.3`: a CI trigger and asset lookup per package, and one GitHub Release per package version) | The tag names a release of the repository, not a version of a package, so a package version is never skipped. The records already map each version to its tag, so the manifest, the publish job and the verification needed no change. |
| D-367 | **`GET /model/info` is allowed by default on `litellm` endpoints (owner, 2026-11-18; P5-HARN Part C).** goose's `litellm` provider (used for `openai_chat` endpoints) sends `GET /model/info` at every start. It is added to the default allowed paths of endpoints of kind `litellm` only: that one path, `GET` only. The gateway forwards it like an inference path, with the placeholder replaced by the endpoint's key. Other endpoint kinds and other metadata paths stay refused (`llm_path_not_permitted`). An endpoint's `allowed_paths` only adds paths (`06`, `08`), so this default cannot be removed per endpoint. | Keep refusing (one refused request row per goose turn, goose tolerates it); refuse but hide that row from the request log (a logging special case for one path) | The owner chose it. It is read-only metadata on the endpoint the agent already uses. Every agent on that endpoint can now read LiteLLM's model metadata, not only goose; that was accepted. |
| D-368 | **The gateway has a wire-level debug log (owner, 2026-11-18; FIX-166).** The tracing target `aurora_gateway::wire`, at `debug`, is off at the default `info`; `telemetry.log_level: "info,aurora_gateway::wire=debug"` turns it on. For every forwarded request it logs three events (received, forwarded, response), each with the `request_id`. They show what the gateway received, what it sent upstream after injection and any body re-wrap, and what came back: HTTP version and negotiated protocol, method, authority, path, query parameter **names** with value lengths, header names, body framing (end of stream, size hint, `Content-Length`/`Transfer-Encoding`), status, latency, and whether the gateway replaced the answer. Injected parameters and headers are marked `<injected>`. Values are logged only for a fixed allow-list of framing and identification headers (`content-length`, `transfer-encoding`, `content-type`, `content-encoding`, `accept`, `accept-encoding`, `user-agent`, `te`, `connection`, `expect`, `upgrade`, `host`, `server`, `via`). Credential and injected values, body bytes, raw query values and every other header value are never logged (SEC-01; a scrub test proves it). Also decided: a request body the gateway read to its end is forwarded with the framing it arrived with (empty stays empty, a whole body gets `Content-Length`). | Log full requests at `trace` (values and bodies in a log, against SEC-01 and `06 §Telemetry`); a per-agent debug switch in the dashboard (a new setting and route for an operator diagnosis); `request_log` columns for framing (it is the tenant's activity record, not a wire trace) | The cern-maps 500s (FIX-166) took an owner session and live probing to locate, though one forwarded-request line would have shown it. Names, lengths and framing are enough to find protocol faults, and they hold no tenant data. |
| D-369 | **A connector update that widens its manifest keeps the connector running at the approved level (owner, 2026-11-18; FIX-167; amends `13 §3`, `§3.1`).** Some registrations announce a manifest that widens an approved baseline (a new capability, setup field or public callback). The connector stays `active` and its integrations keep being served. Until an instance admin approves, the core grants, routes and assigns only what the **approved** manifest declares (one effective-manifest accessor; SEC-134). The update shows as pending, with its widening. A first registration (no baseline) still waits in `pending_approval`. Unchanged and narrowed manifests are still accepted automatically. | Keep stopping the connector until approval and only announce it louder; auto-approve widenings of built-in connector types signed by the project key (looser than built-in app packages, which wait when they widen) | The Mattermost bridge went silent after an upgrade (FIX-158 and P4-UTOK added capabilities). Every new capability of a built-in connector would do the same. The core cannot verify which binary runs (`13 §3`), so the approval always covered what the core grants, not the code. Serving at the approved level keeps everything the approval protected and removes the outage. |
| D-370 | **Instance admins are told when something needs them (owner, 2026-11-18; FIX-167).** `GET /v1/admin/attention` lists, computed on read: connectors waiting for approval (first registration or a pending update), package versions in `pending_approval`, addon sources whose signing key changed, host claims waiting for a winner (all decisions), and, not as decisions, managed connectors that are down and ownerless organizations. The sidebar's Administration entry shows the count. Administration › Overview opens with **Needs your attention**. Each decision item, when it first appears, sends one e-mail and one Web Push to each instance admin (the approval mailer and push paths, deduplicated per item and admin in a table, log-only without SMTP, a per-user e-mail opt-out). Items that are not decisions are never mailed. | Dashboard only (the owner checked the dashboard only after the bridge broke); a daily digest e-mail; e-mail on health items too (they flap) | The owner learned of the waiting Mattermost update only by going to look. A count where admins already click, plus one message per new decision, is enough without becoming noise. |
| D-371 | **The harnesses in the web terminal run with approvals and their own sandbox off, like a turn (owner, 2026-11-18; FIX-169; amends D-247, C-750, C-1015).** The `claude`, `codex` and `goose` wrappers start their CLI with the same approval and sandbox settings the adapters use for a turn: Claude Code in bypass-permissions mode, Codex with `approval_policy = "never"` and `sandbox_mode = "danger-full-access"`, goose with `GOOSE_MODE=auto`. `opencode` (`permission: allow`) and `pi` (never asks) already did. It is a default: a mode or approval choice the person gives explicitly on the command line (goose: `GOOSE_MODE` in the environment) is kept. Nothing about egress changes. | Keep the CLIs' own prompts in the terminal; the person runs `claude --dangerously-skip-permissions` by hand each time; a dashboard switch | SEC-66 already turns the prompts off for turns because the Aurora sandbox and the gateway are the boundaries. The terminal has a turn's privilege (D-314 withdrawn), and the person at it already has a shell. The prompts guard nothing the terminal does not already allow, and Codex's own sandbox cannot nest in the container. |
| D-372 | **A hosted agent on a LiteLLM endpoint works without a configured model: the first chat model its key serves, in LiteLLM's order (owner, 2026-11-18; FIX-170; amends D-136, C-500, C-878).** For an agent without a usable override, the default selection on a `litellm` endpoint takes the endpoint `default_model` only when the key's live listing contains it (or the listing could not be read); otherwise it takes the **first** model of the key's `GET /v1/models` listing, in LiteLLM's order, that is allowed and is a chat model (by `/model/info`'s `model_info.mode` when known), `source = "key_listing"`. Only the public `model_name` is a candidate. The listing is that of the key the gateway injects for the agent on that endpoint. Readiness warns `default_model_not_served`, `override_not_served` and `role_not_served`; an override is never re-pointed. `no_default_model` remains only when nothing resolves. The `no_default_model` chat links open the failing agent's model field. Other providers are unchanged. | Keep D-136 (never pick from the listing; a missing default fails turns); a per-namespace `default_model` keyed by LiteLLM team (option (b)); prefer the model the key used most recently (no help on a first turn) | Production: `aigw.cern.ch` serves several LiteLLM namespaces whose model names differ, so one endpoint-wide `default_model` sent `hosted_vllm/glm53-flash-int4` with a key whose namespace calls it `glm-5.3-flash-int4`, and LiteLLM answered 403 (`llm_auth`); without the default, a new user's first message failed `no_default_model` with a broken link. The owner wants an agent to answer right away. The operator controls LiteLLM's order, which answers D-136's "order is not meaningful"; `/model/info`'s mode answers "mixes chat and embedding models". |
| D-373 | **The dashboard shows what each audit row recorded (owner, 2026-11-18; FIX-171).** Every row on the instance, organization and workspace audit pages opens a **Details** view. It shows a settings `change` list as before/after per key and list entry, other `{from, to}`, `{before, after}` and `{old, new}` pairs as before → after, the rest of `metadata` as labelled values, and the row's source, IP and ids. All of it renders as text, and secrets appear only as the markers already recorded. The audit pages read their filters from the URL and write them back. The instance-settings e-mail links to that day's `instance_settings.` rows. No API, schema or database change: the same readers already receive `metadata`. | Show only the settings diff; a per-action wording table; a list-by-id route so the e-mail opens the exact row | The settings e-mail said "the before and after values are in the audit log", and they were not visible: the API returns them, but no audit view rendered `metadata`, and the e-mail's `?action=` was ignored. The owner finds the audit log too thin to be useful. Most of the missing detail is already recorded, so showing it is the cheap first step. Recording more per domain can follow. |
| D-374 | **Harnesses are picked by name, never typed; Create agent offers none (owner, 2026-11-18; FIX-172).** Administration › Agents › Defaults › Hosted agents shows `hosted.enabled_harnesses` as a checkbox group of the known harnesses by display name. A harness no registered runner offers cannot be ticked (C-1028 `agents.extra.offered_harnesses`). `hosted.default_harness` is a select of the ticked ones, and unticking the default moves it to the first one still ticked. With one harness enabled, the agent's Instructions tab shows its name as text, and with more it keeps the P4-HAR dropdown. Create agent offers no harness: a new agent takes the default, and changing it is an advanced setting on Instructions. The API keeps accepting `harness` on create. Stored values, Helm keys and validation are unchanged. | A combobox that still accepts typed ids; a harness step in Create agent; making `hosted.default_harness` an enumeration type (that would break `fake` in development) | The owner found the harness settings to be text boxes where an admin had to know exact ids such as `goose`. The agent picker is hidden while one harness is enabled, which since D-363 is the default, so it looks like a text box too. The server already rejected wrong ids, so this is about usability, not safety. The owner considers changing a harness an advanced feature, so it stays out of creation. |
| D-375 | **Every secret field is one `SecretInput` with Show / Hide (owner, 2026-11-18; FIX-173).** Every write-only value in the dashboard is entered through the shared `SecretInput` primitive (`web/src/components/ui/secret-input.tsx`): the ICS link, the CalDAV app password, every app, connector and integration `secret` field, OAuth client secrets, secret settings in Replace…, the OIDC client secret, secret store credentials, personal access tokens, webhook secrets, and `ValueInput`. It starts hidden. A Show / Hide button named after the field reveals what the person typed. It hides again when the dialog closes, when the form clears the value, or when the field is disabled. A test fails on any other password input in `web/src`. Stored values are never sent to the page (SEC-12 is unchanged), so only typed text can be shown. | Showing the ICS link in clear by default (consistent hiding is safer when sharing a screen; Show is one click); a copy button (the value is what was just pasted); a timer that hides it again | The owner wants to check that what was pasted or typed is what they intended. Eight of the nine hand-written secret inputs had no way to reveal it, and the owner asked for one common renderer. |
| D-376 | **Duplicated code is found and replaced by shared helpers in two parallel lanes, measured in lines removed, with no change in behaviour (owner, 2026-11-18; P5-DEDUP-1, P5-DEDUP-2).** P5-DEDUP-1 covers Rust and P5-DEDUP-2 the dashboard. Each runs in its own fresh lane. Each first finds duplication in parallel, with read-only work only: an exact-version `jscpd` scan run through `pnpm dlx` and never added to the repo, plus up to five explorers each sweeping one slice for repeated shapes. Each then commits a ranked catalogue and fixes it one cluster family per commit. A cluster qualifies with 3 or more copies, or 2 copies saving at least 40 lines. Copies that differ on purpose are reported, not unified. Copies that differ in a way that looks like a bug go to the owner. Routes, bodies, error codes, OpenAPI, SQL, migrations, text and accessible names do not change. A web screen may change its look only toward the primitive 14 §Visual design already prescribes. No test case is deleted or weakened. The test counts must not drop. Clusters touching authorization, secrets, crypto, the gateway's decide path, sign-in, approvals or access go last, each with a review. Both lanes yield to other lanes' files, and the Orchestrator merges them every ~5 batches. | One lane for both sides; a codebase-wide automated rewrite; `macro_rules!`-first sharing in Rust; adding a clone detector to CI (it can be proposed after the lanes report) | The owner expects much more duplication like the nine secret inputs FIX-173 found. The goal is fewer lines. Behaviour must not change, because the size of the work makes silent drift the main risk. |
| D-377 | **`sandbox-min` is built on the AlmaLinux 10 minimal image; bootc images are not container bases (owner, 2026-11-18; FIX-174).** A new build argument `SANDBOX_MIN_BASE_IMAGE` (default `docker.io/library/almalinux:10-minimal`, digest-pinned, guarded like `SANDBOX_BASE_IMAGE`) is the base of the `sandbox-min` target only. One script, `images/sandbox/os-setup.sh`, installs the same package list with `dnf` or `microdnf` in both the `os` and the new `os-min` stage, so the two cannot drift. The production `sandbox` image and the distroless service images are unchanged. The change merges only if the measured image is smaller. | AlmaLinux 10 **bootc** images as service or sandbox bases (a bootable OS image: kernel, firmware, systemd; its read-only `/usr`, signed updates and rollback protect only a booted host; as a container base it is larger, has more packages to patch and expects systemd as PID 1 where the supervisor is PID 1); `almalinux:10-micro` (no package manager, not in the Docker official library); moving the production `sandbox` now (decided after the measurement) | The owner asked whether bootc would make the bases more secure. It would not for containers; bootc fits the runner hosts, if the operator manages them. The minimal image is 31 MB compressed against 69 MB (Docker Hub, 2026-11-18) and has fewer packages, which helps e2e pulls and image scans. |
| D-378 | **The sandbox image is pre-pulled on every sandbox node (owner, 2026-11-18; FIX-175).** On Kubernetes the chart renders a DaemonSet `<release>-sandbox-prepull` in the sandbox namespace (`runner.prepull.enabled`, default `true`): one container of the effective sandbox image running `sleep infinity`, with the sandbox Pods' node selector, tolerations and pull secrets, no runtime class, the sandbox Pods' restricted security context, tiny resources and no `aurora.*` labels (the runner's reconcile never sees it). The image and placement are computed once in `_helpers.tpl` for both the runner ConfigMap and the DaemonSet. Its Pods count against the sandbox namespace quota. Podman already pulls the image when the runner registers. | An init container plus a `pause` container (the image is then unused and image garbage collection can remove it; a second image to mirror); the runner pulling through the Kubernetes API (it has no node access); setting `imagePullPolicy: Always` (slower); a pre-upgrade hook pulling the next image (later, if needed) | The first sandbox on each node after an install, upgrade or node replacement waited for the full sandbox image (Chromium, Node.js, five harnesses) inside its start. A running container keeps the image on the node. |
| D-379 | **Warm starts: on the first composer keystroke, on by default, first in phase 6; a pool only after measuring (owner, 2026-11-18; answers Q-P6-WARM-1..4 of P6-WARMPOOL).** (1) A warm-up starts on the first keystroke in an agent's composer, not on opening the chat. (2) `hosted.warm_on_intent` is on by default; an instance admin can turn it off. (3) Nothing ships in phase 5: start-time measurement (Part 1) and warm-on-intent (Part 2) are specified and built together at the beginning of phase 6. (4) Pre-created homes (Part 3) and a true generic pool (homes delivered over the network) are decided only after Part 1's measurements. | Warm on opening the chat (boots sandboxes for people reading history); off by default; the measurement first as a phase-5 FIX (the coordinator's recommendation); committing now to no generic pool ever | Typing is the clearest sign a message is coming, and the start then runs while the person writes. The owner wants both parts to land together at the start of phase 6, and the pool question answered by data, not assumptions. |
| D-380 | **UX review 4 and its fixes: the "Aurora UX fixes" artifact is the reference for P5-POLISH (owner, 2026-11-19).** The owner walked the finished redesign and found it rough: text cut off, a primary button with no readable text, dead ends and pages full of operator detail. The review documents 100 screens (<https://claude.ai/artifact/33oHaXQpCU3oSVRVmgtY5Z>). Thirty proposals with a before screenshot and an after mockup in Aurora's own tokens (<https://claude.ai/artifact/WXbcpcrkbCLuxMSurpkuQW>, version `1795034441-5823`, IDs A1..F3) were commented on by the owner (16 threads, all resolved into the artifact) and accepted ("time to prepare the briefs"). The briefs P5-POLISH-1..11 build them. **Precedence:** the brief, then the owner's resolved comments (as the artifact now shows them), then the mockup. Text in a mockup is the target where a brief rebuilds that screen; elsewhere it is illustrative. Workers cannot open artifacts, so a read-only copy with PNG renders of every mockup is kept at `/home/alex/orca/workspaces/aurora/design-ux-fixes/` (`after/<id>.png`, `img/<id>.webp` for the before crops, `index.html`, `review/`). A **wording pass** over the whole dashboard follows separately and is not part of P5-POLISH. | Editing the D-354 canvas instead (its boards are the redesign's reference and stay so; the fixes artifact records what the build got wrong and the owner's later choices); one brief per proposal (thirty briefs, too many shared files) | The redesign's briefs were right about most things, but several screens were built differently from their boards, and the owner made new choices while commenting (agent tabs, builtin apps, health lines). One reference with before and after keeps implementers and the final review aligned. |
| D-381 | **Apps shipped in the Aurora image arrive approved, and so do their updates in a release (owner, 2026-11-19, comment on E5: "Yes, they should"; P5-POLISH-3).** Release images already ship the builtin packages signed by the project key in `plugins.builtin_dir` and activate them at first install (D-75). This decision extends that: (a) a project-signed builtin is also approved at every **upgrade**, including one whose manifest widens permissions, with an audit row `plugin.auto_approved` naming the version; (b) the dev and kind images, which today copy the packages unsigned into `plugins.dir` where each waits as `pending_approval` (D-326(d)), load them as builtins too, so a dev install behaves like a release one. Unsigned builtins are accepted only where `plugins.allow_unsigned` is already on (dev values), never in a release. The organization switch (D-344, E3) still decides whether people may connect an app. Uploaded packages, packages from `plugins.dir` and packages from an addon source (P5-ASRC) still wait for an instance admin's review (SEC-136). Administration › Overview loses its approval step (five steps). | Keeping every package pending until reviewed (12 builtin apps, about 60 clicks before anyone can connect anything); a bulk "Approve all" button (still a step nobody needs for code that shipped with the release) | The image's packages are part of the release the admin chose to install; reviewing them again adds work and no safety. Third-party code keeps its review (SEC-136). |
| D-382 | **Agent page: Chat, Triggers and Computer are tabs; Behaviour and Access move to the … menu (owner, 2026-11-19, comment on C4; P5-POLISH-6; amends D-338's agent sections).** Behaviour and Access are configuration, not things a person does with the agent. The … menu lists Behaviour ("Name, instructions, model, memory, skills"), Access ("Apps, accounts and the AI key it uses"), a separator, **Freeze <Agent>** ("Pauses it until you unfreeze it") and **Delete <Agent>…** (danger). On a configuration page no tab is selected and the … button is marked; under the header a back link "Chat", the page name and one sentence name it. Deep links (`?tab=instructions`, `?tab=access`, …) keep working. On a phone the same menu holds them. | Five tabs (today); a separate settings page per agent | The tabs are what a person does with the agent; the owner asked for configuration to step aside. |
| D-383 | **A health line says only what a real call proved (owner, 2026-11-19, comment on C1: "There's nothing worse than getting an error and see the interface telling you that it works"; P5-POLISH-2, -3).** An account's line is "Worked <time> ago" from the last successful call an agent made through it, "Failed <time> ago: <reason in words>" with its fix (Reconnect, Sign in again) from the first failed call until a call succeeds again, and "Not used yet" before any call. A setting, a saved key or a passed **Test** never shows as "Works"; a test shows as "Test passed <time> ago" until real use replaces it. The same rule applies to AI keys and to every place that shows an account's state (rows, the agent header, Overview). Real use and tests are recorded separately (C-1033: today one `last_check_*` pair holds both). Amends P5-UX-14's wording (C-944). | "Works" whenever no failure is known (today); the test result as the health | A green "Works" next to an error the person just saw destroys trust in every other status. |
| D-384 | **Amber means "you can act on this" (P5-POLISH-2; amends `14 §Visual design` › Redesign canvas, "amber for anything that needs a person").** The pending (amber) styles are allowed only on an item with an action for the viewer. Who owns or locks a value is said with a neutral **lock pill** ("Set by Users", "Set in Helm") whose hover names who can change it; there is no "Ask alice" line under every row. A design guard flags pending styles without an action. At most one chip per card; the kind of a thing is shown by its icon, state by a dot and a sentence-case line. | Amber for ownership (today: "SET BY YOUR ORGANIZATION", "HELM", "MANAGED BY HELM") | When amber also marks things nobody can act on, people stop reading it. |
| D-385 | **The model stays out of the agent's Access tab and the role warnings move to the endpoint (P5-POLISH-3, -6; amends P4-LLM-6 and FIX-170 placement).** The agent's model is chosen on Behaviour › Instructions (one select: "Workspace default · <model>" or a model). Model roles, effort and the agent program are under a collapsed **Advanced** there. The Access tab has no Model readiness block: the AI key is one row ("Daneel thinks with <model> through this key"), and a real problem shows as that row's health line and as the chat header's state ("Can't answer"). A missing role on an endpoint whose agents may ask for one is flagged once, on the endpoint's row in Administration › AI models, not per agent. | Keeping the block for owners (today); hiding the warnings entirely | A new person cannot act on "Large is not set"; the admin who can set roles never sees it. |
| D-386 | **Ask an admin to set up an app (P5-POLISH-4, SEC-135).** Where an app needs an organization owner or admin first (no sign-in app registered, or the app off for the organization), the journey and the Apps tab offer **Ask <Name>**. The request is per organization and app; a second person's ask joins it ("Bob and Carol want Google Calendar"). It appears in the **Needs you** card of the workspace Overview for every organization owner and admin, on the app's row in Organization › Apps & accounts › Apps, and as a notification (e-mail and push through the existing notifier, one per request, folded per hour). It resolves by itself when the app becomes ready for that organization; each requester is then notified and their journey offers the app. A requester may withdraw (Undo); an admin may decline ("Not now"), which tells the requesters in one sentence. | E-mail only; a free-text request field (no free text: a fixed request carries no content an admin must moderate) | The journey's dead end ("Ask alice") gave no way to ask and the admin never heard. |
| D-387 | **Packages describe themselves for people (P5-POLISH-3).** The manifest gains, all optional: `summary` (one plain sentence of at most 100 characters, shown on cards, never cut); per access level `permission_hints` (the token permissions to tick for that level, by the app's own names, e.g. "Contents · Read"); `first_prompt` (one suggested first question, shown on the journey's Done step and opened as a draft in a new conversation). Every builtin package fills all three. | Truncating `description` (today); hard-coding hints in the web | Cards cut sentences mid-word, the token step did not say what to tick, and Done gave no next step. |
| D-388 | **The connect journey is built from server readiness and keeps its shape (P5-POLISH-5; amends P5-UX-3).** The App step groups apps by `readiness` (C-1029): Ready (alphabetical), Already connected (an account exists that this agent cannot use yet: **Give access**), Needs an admin first (the reason and **Ask <Name>**); apps the organization switched off are not listed; a search field filters all groups. The dialog has a fixed height on desktop (600 px; only its body scrolls) and is a full-screen sheet below 640 px; the footer is Back on the left and the step's one action on the right on every step. The title is "<App> for <Agent>" (the app step: "Give <Agent> an app"). Typed values survive a failed connect, secrets included (kept in the page only, masked, with Show), and the error sits under the field. Done names the account and the access level and offers **Try it in chat** with the package's `first_prompt` as an unsent draft. | Grouping from a per-tile plan fetched on focus (today, which listed every app as ready); clearing secrets after a failure | The journey is the main way people give agents apps; every step of it should be honest and steady. |
| D-389 | **One switcher for organization and workspace; personal workspaces named from the viewer's side (owner, 2026-11-19, comments on D4; P5-POLISH-8).** The rail has one switcher showing the workspace and its organization. A person with one organization and one workspace gets no menu. The menu shows a search field ("Find a workspace or organization", results grouped under Workspaces and Organizations; an organization result opens its workspaces); the current organization's workspaces, at most 5, most recently used first, then "All <n> in <Org>"; other organizations as one row each with their workspace count, opening their workspaces in the same menu with a back arrow; "All <n> organizations" and the "All …" links open the full list page; **New workspace** at the bottom when allowed. The level pill above page titles is dropped wherever the switcher already says where you are. A personal workspace is shown as "Your workspace" to its owner and "<First name>'s workspace" to others; its stored name is unchanged. | Two stacked pickers (today); one flat list of every workspace | Two pickers for one place confused new people, and a flat list does not scale to many organizations. |
| D-390 | **Organization pages have a member view (P5-POLISH-9).** A person who cannot change an organization page's rules sees a sentence of what applies to them and who can change it (`WhoCan` with the owners' names and **Ask**), never the owners' form with disabled controls. This applies to Organization › AI models, Internet access and Agents. | Disabled forms (today) | A member read 2,300 px of checkboxes they could not change. |
| D-391 | **Administration › Overview stays a checklist until setup is done (P5-POLISH-10).** Five steps: Sign-in, Where new people land, AI key, Try it, E-mail (optional). Each step is one row with a summary and one button that opens the real settings page or a dialog; no form is embedded. Progress shows a toast and stays on the checklist; the health view replaces it only when every required step is done (or from a **Health** tab). An optional step never shows Ready while it has open items. | The embedded placement form and the jump to the health view after the AI key (today) | The admin lost their place and never saw "Try it". |
| D-392 | **Settings rows and unavailable features (P5-POLISH-2, -10).** Settings cards span the page container (1080 px); each row has the label and help on the left and the control on the right, stacking below 640 px; booleans are switches; one guide link per card instead of a line under every field; a value managed by Helm shows the neutral lock pill "Set in Helm" and a read-only control. A feature the deployment cannot turn on shows one **Unavailable** card (what is missing, who can fix it, a guide link) and no disabled form. | Half-width forms with a guide line under each field (today) | Settings pages wasted half the screen while cutting their own tables. |
| D-393 | **Dialogs are three rows; CSS element rules live in a layer; one focus ring (P5-POLISH-1).** A dialog is a flex column with a max height: header (title, close, optional stepper), body (the only part that scrolls), footer. No sticky header with negative margins. Below 640 px a dialog may use the `sheet` variant (full screen). Element rules in `globals.css` (`a`, `html`, `body`, headings, `::placeholder`) live in `@layer base` so utilities win. Focus is shown with `:focus-visible` only, one ring; containers that hold a focused field (the composer) change their border instead of adding a ring; menus and popovers have no outline. Inline styles injected by libraries carry the CSP nonce. | Patching each dialog | The sticky header covered the first 24 px of every dialog body (the cut-off step labels) and the unlayered `a` rule blanked every link styled as a primary button. |
| D-394 | **Sandbox processes cannot trace each other (owner, 2026-11-19; FIX-179, SEC-137).** The sandbox's seccomp profile denies `ptrace`, `process_vm_readv` and `process_vm_writev` on both backends (a `Localhost` profile = `RuntimeDefault` plus these denials on Kubernetes, `--security-opt seccomp=` on podman); a pod that cannot get the profile does not start. This closes FIX-151's residual (a same-uid process acting as the harness where the host's `kernel.yama.ptrace_scope` is 0). A debugger inside the sandbox stops working. | Accept the residual; require `ptrace_scope` ≥ 1 on hosts (host configuration Aurora does not control) | The tool-call binding (SEC-121) should not depend on a host sysctl. |
| D-395 | **The gateway's HTTP/1 proxy ends a stalled request body after 60 s of silence with 408 (owner, 2026-11-19; FIX-180, C-1040).** An idle timeout, not a total limit: a slow but moving body continues. On expiry, `408 request_body_timeout` if no response has started, the connection closes, the request-log row records it, and what the decision path held is released. The constant is `GATEWAY_BODY_IDLE_TIMEOUT` in `limits.rs`, not a setting. | No body timeout (a stalled client holds its connection and what the decision path holds); a total-duration limit (cuts legitimate slow uploads) | Found by FIX-177's analysis; the head already has a read timeout. |
| D-396 | **Hardening batch from the P5-DEDUP-1 sweep (owner, 2026-11-19; FIX-181, C-1041).** (a) A frozen agent is answered **423 `frozen`** on every route, including SSH redemption and Files open (were 409). (b) **No invisible formatting characters in any user-set name or label** (`sanitize::is_stripped`, one shared validator, as organisation names already are). (c) **List routes authorise before parsing** pagination. (d) `WorkspaceAuth::can_use()` is removed: a `WorkspaceAuth` exists only for an active member with an effective workspace role, so it was `true` by construction since BE-010 and its ~10 `if !ws.can_use()` branches were dead (the real check is the construction; ITs prove it per route family). (e) The gateway's link and DB lanes match the request lane (`Retry-After` while restarting; the approval-store-unavailable counter and reason); Mattermost's `ct_eq` is constant-time over the longer length; the dev-switch and URL checks apply to relay, terminator and connector. | Leave them (each is small; together they are inconsistencies a reviewer would flag) | The owner's answers to the sweep's findings F1, F2, F4–F7, F10, F11. |
| D-397 | **A package can mark a write tool "ask first" by default (owner, 2026-11-19; P5-TOOLASK, C-1042, C-1043).** A catalogue tool may carry `approval: ask` (write group only; additive in contract 1). The gateway treats it as Ask unless an explicit workspace or organisation rule allows or blocks that tool (explicit Allow wins over the package default; Block wins over everything); the card says the app asks before this action; the policy simulator and the access settings show it. `caldav`'s `send_scheduling_message` uses it. | Keep the workaround (a workspace adds the tool to its ask list, `09`); a hard requirement no workspace can lift | Tools that act on other people (meeting invitations, webhooks) should ask by default without each workspace having to know. |
| D-398 | **Connectors say whether they have an icon (owner, 2026-11-19; FIX-183, C-1044).** `ConnectorAdmin` carries `has_icon`, derived from the same source the icon route serves; the dashboard requests an icon only when it is true and shows the monogram tile otherwise. | Answer 204 from the icon route for a connector without an icon | The dashboard knows before asking, as it does for apps; no request that is known to fail. |
| D-399 | **The API gaps the redesign found are closed in phase 5 (owner, 2026-11-19; P5-APIGAP-1..4).** (1) `POST /v1/agents/{agent}/sandbox/wake` wakes a sleeping computer and `SandboxView.idle_stop_after_secs` says when it sleeps (C-1045, C-1046). (2) A person can ask an admin for a chat platform or an AI key, not only an app: D-386's request gains a subject (C-1047). (3) Members see their key's default model and organization admins see which key new agents get (read-only; C-1048, C-1049). (4) Workspace People show when each person joined (C-1050) and a workspace owner can suspend one person in one workspace, with the organization-wide suspension's semantics scoped to that workspace; a suspended binding grants nothing (C-1051, SEC-138). | Leave the placeholders and remove the controls from the designs | The redesign's controls were accepted (D-380); the owner chose to add what they need. |
| D-400 | **Google Workspace and Microsoft 365 ask before sending mail or invitations, by default and changeably (owner, 2026-11-19; FIX-184; applies D-397).** Their write tools that send mail, or invitations/updates to attendees, carry `approval: ask`. A workspace lifts it per tool with **Allowed** in the actions table, an organization with a rule naming the tool (D-397); Block still wins. Other builtins are unchanged. | No default (today); a hard Ask no one can lift | Sending in someone's name to other people should be confirmed unless a workspace or organization decides otherwise. |
| D-401 | **Suspending someone in an organization stops their running turns (owner, 2026-11-19; FIX-185).** When an organization member is suspended (`PATCH /v1/orgs/{org}/members/{user}` `status: suspended`, and every other path that suspends an org member), their queued and live speaker turns in every workspace of that organization end `aborted`/`user_suspended` with a notice, in the same transaction, as SCIM deprovisioning and the workspace suspension (P5-APIGAP-4) already do. Restoring starts nothing. | Leave turns running until they finish (today) | A suspended person's agent should not keep acting for them. |
| D-402 | **The agent knows who it is working for (owner, 2026-11-20; P5-PERSON, C-1052..C-1055, SEC-139).** Every turn except eval turns starts with a turn context block built at dispatch and never stored. It gives the local time, the turn's person (the speaker, or a schedule's creator; none for webhook, watch, peer and eval turns) and the door. A scheduled run says whose schedule it is and where its answer goes. (a) For a person with an Aurora account the block gives their display name, verified e-mail, cached chat handle and time zone. The e-mail is left out in group spaces, because their other readers may not be workspace members (a coordinator refinement of the owner's answer). (b) No person-written "About you" note in this brief. (c) `send_message` accepts `to: "me"`, the turn's person on the turn's platform. It is sent without approval unless proactive messaging is `off`. (d) A personal time zone (`users.timezone`), filled in from the browser when unset and editable in Account; the workspace's zone is the fallback. | Leave the speaker label as the only identity; e-mail always shown, group spaces included; an "About you" note; `me` under `ask` held for approval each time; the workspace time zone only | A daily scheduled message never arrived: the run had no idea who "me" was. "From my office" questions failed for the same reason. The person the turn runs for is already defined for calendar invitations (SEC-108); this reuses it. |
| D-403 | **The agent page header has no empty space and its lines meet (owner, 2026-11-20; FIX-186, FIX-187).** (a) The section switcher (Chat · Triggers · Computer) is as wide as its tabs: no empty track after the last tab; on a phone the tabs share the row equally. (b) The collapsed status strip puts its expand button in a 64 px top cell whose bottom border runs the strip's full width and continues the thread header's bottom border, as the expanded panel's header already does. | A fixed-width switcher track (today); a short centred divider at a different height (today) | Visual review: an empty stretch after Computer, and two horizontal lines that miss each other when the rail is collapsed. |
| D-404 | **The terminal's `goose` and `pi` start ready to use (owner, 2026-11-20; FIX-189; Pi added by the owner the same day, FIX-190 step 5: a terminal `settings.json` from the turn's renderer, written the same way, and the same "no model" sentence).** The `goose` wrapper creates a minimal `config.yaml` under its terminal root when there is none, with only non-secret keys: provider, model, host and telemetry off. goose 1.59.2 opens its provider wizard whenever that file is missing. An existing file is the person's and stays. With no model for goose and no arguments, the wrapper prints why and exits instead of opening the wizard; with arguments (for example `goose configure`), it passes them through. | Leave the wizard (today); write the file on every start, overwriting the person's | A person in the terminal cannot and should not choose an LLM provider: the gateway already serves the agent's model. |
| D-405 | **UX review 5 and its fixes (owner, 2026-11-20): the "Aurora UX Review 5" artifact is the reference for P5-REFINE-1..20.** The review walked every page while configuring and using every feature (511 screens, 119 findings each with a Before and an After mockup, 10 priorities, 10 themes, the runner bug, 12 blind spots, and "The layer above": 16 recommendations by a second reviewer). Artifact <https://claude.ai/artifact/1YJcRA7Q84864pkim44Qnt> (version 1795168465-42c7, the owner's comments applied; finding anchors `#f-<key>`, e.g. `#f-access-1`). Workers cannot open artifacts: the read-only copy is `/home/alex/orca/workspaces/aurora/design-ux-review-5/` (`findings.md`, `priorities-themes.md`, `layer-above.md`, `after/<key>.png` dark and `-lt.png` light renders, `after/src/<key>.html`, `before/`, `screens/`, `notes/`). Precedence: the brief, then the owner's decisions (D-405..D-419 and the artifact's comments), then the After mockup, then the finding's fix text; yellow dashed notes in mockups are commentary. P5-REFINE-20 verifies every finding, priority, theme, recommendation and decision against its mockup on a throwaway cluster and **sends every difference back to the owning brief, pass after pass, until all match** or the owner accepts a difference in writing. | Briefs without mockups; one big brief; a single review pass (P5-POLISH-11 left 10 minor differences) | The owner: "make sure the briefs include links to the artifact/screenshots/mockups so they can reproduce them reliably the first time" and "if they don't, they need to be sent back and iterated on until they do". |
| D-406 | **Three altitudes: Use, Set up, Expert, with one per-person switch (owner, 2026-11-20; review rec. 1).** Every control has exactly one altitude. Use: everyone (chat, approvals, the agent's summary and timeline, Overview, Give an app, the access choice, Files). Set up: workspace owners and anyone with the switch on (instructions, memory, skills, automations, people, settings, rules, outgoing-webhook templates, model choice). Expert: the switch only, and instance admins in Administration (tests, other agents, terminal, SSH, previews, BYO, model roles/effort/program, raw cron, Technical details, the request-log table, export, CLI/API snippets, delivery ids). **"Show more controls" is per person**, stored server-side beside `welcome_seen` so it follows them across browsers; the role still sets the floor, so the switch never shows what the role may not use. Hidden controls collapse to one line "n more controls · Show". | A role-only rule; an "expert mode" with separate pages; per-browser storage | One system instead of five ad-hoc Advanced folds; an expert member can still reach Terminal and cron. |
| D-407 | **One vocabulary, renamed at once (owner, 2026-11-20; rec. 2 and rec. 15).** All renames in the review's vocabulary table land together: Automations (was Triggers; kinds "On a schedule", "When something arrives", "In a chat channel"), Tests / Grader / Checked by (Evals / Judge / Checked as), Instructions (Behaviour's page, and "Brief"), Apps & access (Access), Other agents (Peers), Your rules, Restrict further…, Try a request, Sign-in app everywhere, Chat platform / Chat platform host, Web address, Remove this message's content… (Redact…), Language <Agent> remembers in, Came with this workspace, Set for you by <who>; outcome words Received / Refused: … / Duplicate, ignored and Answered / Skipped by the gate / Failed / Running; Paused / On for automations. **Pause / Resume replaces Freeze in the UI for agents, workspaces and organizations** (API codes such as `frozen` unchanged); one word per rung: Pause, Turn off, Suspend, Remove from, Rotate / Promote, Delete. **Text that sends someone to an administrator names the role, not a person** ("Ask an admin", "Managed by your organization's admins"; the request goes to all of them). A person's display name comes from the sign-in's `name` claim. **"What moved" is removed entirely** (banner, menu entry, the renames table): there are no users yet. Amends D-338's notice. | Renames one by one with notices; keep Freeze for organizations; name the admin who set things up | The owner accepted every rename and Pause for organizations; admins can be several people. |
| D-408 | **Agent page: a labelled Settings button; status follows the computer (owner, 2026-11-20; rec. 3; amends D-382).** The tab bar is Chat · Automations · Computer; configuration stays out of the tabs but behind a labelled **Settings** button (Instructions, Apps & access, Memory, Skills, Tests, Other agents; Pause and Delete at its bottom), not an unlabelled "…". The header's status **follows the agent's computer**: "Starting its computer…" while it boots (Terminal and Files show the same line and connect by themselves once it is up), **Ready** only once the computer answers its readiness check, Asleep (wakes on the next message or when Terminal opens), Working while it runs. The status rail has text labels and the headings Needs you · Doing now · What <Agent> did. | An unlabelled "…" (D-382); a Ready that reflects only the agent record | The walkers hunted for the "…"; the owner saw Ready while the terminal still could not connect. |
| D-409 | **Several chats per person and one thread per automation run (owner, 2026-11-20; amends D-230's one direct conversation per person and agent).** A person may have several direct conversations with an agent, each starting with fresh history: New chat, Rename, an auto-title from the first message; privacy is unchanged (only the person and workspace owners see them). Each schedule run and each webhook delivery gets **its own conversation**, folded under its automation in the conversation list and linked from Runs and Deliveries. D-230's search scope covers the new conversations (the speaker's own direct conversations, and non-direct ones the speaker can see). Chat-platform-origin schedule runs keep posting to their origin thread (FIX-192). | One direct chat (today); a run divider inside one thread | The only fresh start today was Delete, which destroys history; endless trigger threads hide results. |
| D-410 | **Policy presets Open / Balanced / Locked down, Open by default (owner, 2026-11-20; rec. 6).** One choice card above the Administration and organization policy pages writes the underlying settings; editing a field by hand shows "Custom · based on Open"; a Helm-locked key shows as "Open (2 settings set in Helm)". A fresh install starts on **Open**; the setup checklist shows a row "What agents may do · Open · Change". Confirm dialogs describe changes in sentences (D-341 strings), never key names. | Balanced by default; no presets | The owner chose Open; a preset reads in one word where a dozen fields did not. |
| D-411 | **People create agent links (owner, 2026-11-20; rec. 12).** An agent cannot know which other agents exist or whom to ask, so links are made by people: Settings › Other agents › **Link an agent** opens a picker of the agents the person can see and asks what the link may do; the link is also how an agent learns the other exists. Needs a create route for people (today only agents request links). SEC-142. | Links only requested by agents (today) | The owner: "a person has to be able to create them". |
| D-412 | **Members see the computer's phases, not its machinery (owner, 2026-11-20; rec. 11).** Members see phases in words (Off, Starting, On, Stopping, Failed; the raw phase such as `waiting_supervisor` as a secondary line at Expert altitude). The sandbox image and the runner's address are instance-admin only (API and UI); this narrows D-178 Q-R5-5, which also let workspace owners see them (SEC-141). | Everything to members (today); nothing to members | The owner's answer to the open question. |
| D-413 | **Purpose templates live in the web bundle (owner, 2026-11-20; rec. 5).** Templates (name suggestion, 6–10 lines of instructions, a suggested first message, optionally a schedule) are data in `web/src/lib/onboarding/templates.ts`; no API route and not organization-editable for now. A template sets instructions, never access. | Organization-editable templates with a route | Fast and enough until an organization asks. |
| D-414 | **Starter keys reach every hosted agent; no starter key has a clear path (owner, 2026-11-20).** An organization key marked starter is granted to every new hosted agent, not only a person's first, and to existing agents when the key is added, with a visible line "Given to Daneel and 1 other agent". With **no** starter key: Try it isn't offered, setup step 3 stays "Add an AI key so agents can answer", and an agent's chat says "<Agent> has no AI model yet" with Add a key (admins) or Ask an admin (everyone else); agents created before a key get it once it is added. | Starter grant to a person's first agent only (today); grant silently | A second agent was created dead; the owner asked what happens without a key. |
| D-415 | **One live timeline per agent (owner, 2026-11-20; rec. 4).** "What <Agent> can do, is doing and did", in sentences from one label table: answers, app and website calls with their decision, approvals, runs, deliveries, and computer steps (commands run, files written, with a link to Files). The rail's "What <Agent> did" is its last rows; the workspace Activity page lists the agents' timelines; today's request-log table becomes the Expert "Technical view". It updates **live** over the chat's existing stream (not polled): new rows at the top, in-progress rows ("Running…", "Waiting for you") change to their outcome in place, and "3 new · Show" appears when scrolled. | Six places with six vocabularies (today); polling | Trust needs one place that answers "what did my agent do". |
| D-416 | **The runner re-decides its stream listener; an open session keeps the computer awake (UX review 5's bug; computer-1).** The runner opens its stream port (4108) from the configuration it receives at every registration and settings change, not only the first, so turning the terminal, SSH or previews on with `helm upgrade` works without restarting the runner. An open terminal or SSH session counts as activity, as the "Longest a terminal keeps a computer awake" setting promises; if the computer restarts anyway, the terminal prints "The computer restarted at <time>; your running commands were stopped." | Document a runner restart after enabling the terminal; keep the idle stop blind to sessions | Found on the review cluster: Terminal, Files and SSH stayed dead; a 30 s idle stop killed a running command. |
| D-417 | **Requests and attention: one feed per level (owner, 2026-11-20; priority 6, rec. 7).** Workspace, organization and Administration each serve the same attention shape (kind, sentence, detail, since, actors, one action, a done state kept 30 days), rendered by one Needs-you component on Overview, in the rail and as a count next to the level's name for whoever can act. Asking an admin takes an optional reason; declining takes an optional message the asker sees; resolved and declined requests stay in a history; the asker sees "Waiting for an admin since Tuesday" on their own Overview. Chat-platform asks go to instance admins. Extends D-370 and D-386. | Requests on every workspace Overview (today); no history | Requests vanished, landed in unrelated workspaces, and never reached Administration. |
| D-418 | **Invitation links can be copied (owner, 2026-11-20; org-4).** Organization owners and admins see "Copy invitation link" on a pending invitation, always, and Resend when mail works; without mail the link is no longer only in the server log. SEC-140. | Server log only (today) | An install without mail could not invite anyone. |
| D-419 | **Send-only apps have their own access levels (owner, 2026-11-20; access-1).** An app whose tools only send (outgoing webhook) offers "Ask before each send" and "Send without asking"; Read only is never offered for it, and the Done step names the account ("Dani can now send to Ops alerts, and asks you first."). | Offer the three generic levels (today: Read only fails with "A custom grant needs at least one tool") | The recommended level failed with a backend error. |
| D-420 | **A form field can name the connection (owner, 2026-11-20; FIX-194).** A `fields` method may mark one `type: text` field `store: label`. Its value becomes the connection's label, under the rename's rule (`names::clean`, 120 characters). The label is the agent's only way to tell an app's accounts apart. Precedence: the field when non-empty, else the probe's label, else the hook's, else the app's name. On a reconnect, an empty field keeps the current label, and the form prefills it. ICS, webhook and CalDAV move their hard-coded "the `name` field is the label" rule onto it. aws-keys, sentry and postgresql gain an optional Name. The `cern-rotas` addon gains a required Rota name (0.2.0, after the core ships). | Expose chosen connection fields to the agent (a per-field opt-in shown next to each account in `guide.md`, `tools.md` and `api.json`); keep the hard-coded rule for three apps and tell people to rename afterwards | Nothing needs more than a good name, and the label already reaches the agent everywhere. Showing other fields would widen what the agent sees, change three agent-file formats and need a per-field opt-in. It can still be added on top later if a real need appears. |
| D-421 | **One-time schedules use a "run at" time (owner, 2026-11-20; P5-SCHED-ONCE).** A schedule has either a 5-field `cron` or a `run_at`: a local date-time without an offset, read in `timezone`. It is the earlier instant when clocks go back, refused inside a daylight-saving gap, not before the current minute, at most 5 years ahead, and has no minimum interval. A one-time schedule fires once and is then **Done** (`completed_at`). It is kept with its run and removed 30 days later. Done rows don't count toward the 50-per-agent limit. Setting a new `run_at` re-arms it; Pause/Resume on a Done one is refused (`schedule_completed`). When due while the agent is paused, it waits (one `waiting_paused` run, re-checked every minute) and fires late after Resume, and the turn context says it is late. It is never skipped as busy, and failures never disable it. Changing only the time zone keeps the local wall time. The agent tools take `run_at`, and their time zone default (cron and run_at) becomes the speaker's own zone (`users.timezone`), else the workspace's, so "tomorrow at 9" matches Now in the turn context. The dashboard adds **Every year** (a 5-field cron) and **Once** presets to P5-REFINE-14's row. Cron stays 5 fields. C-1092, C-1094. | 7-field cron (`croner` supports seconds and years) | A cron pinned to a year is still a one-time schedule, and the engine needs one-time rules regardless: what happens after the last fire, and what happens when the agent is paused or busy (skipping to "the next occurrence" would silently lose a reminder). A model writes `2026-11-21T09:00` far more reliably than `0 0 9 21 11 * 2026`. Yearly repetition already fits in 5 fields. |
| D-422 | **A reminder reaches the person where they asked (owner, 2026-11-20; P5-SCHED-ONCE).** A one-time schedule whose origin conversation is the direct conversation of its current creator (not archived, same agent) **runs in that conversation**. The turn uses the schedule door, queues behind an active turn, and gets no separate "finished" notice. The turn context says the creator reads it. Any other run, and every recurring run, keeps its own run thread (D-409); FIX-192's mirror to chat-platform origins is unchanged. When such a run ends `done`, the creator's opted-in browsers get one Web Push (`schedule_answer`; the schedule name only with `show_details`, never the answer). SEC-144, C-1093, C-1095. | Keep the run thread and copy the answer into the origin as a message; rely on `send_message` `to: "me"` (needs a chat platform); a notice only | Running in the chat gives the agent the conversation's history ("remind me to do X"), puts the answer where the person reads it, and lets a reply such as "again in an hour" be a normal turn. A web-only person had no way to be told; the push gives them one. |
| D-423 | **The owner of a personal workspace starts at Use (owner, 2026-11-20; confirms P5-REFINE-3's floor, amends D-406's role floor).** In their own personal workspace a person sees the Use altitude by default, like a member: Overview, Agents and Approvals. "Show more controls" opens Set up and Expert. The owner of a team workspace, organization owners and admins and instance admins keep Set up (instance admins get Expert inside Administration). | Set up, like any workspace owner | A person's own workspace is where a newcomer starts ("the first ten minutes"); the switch is one click away. |
| D-424 | **A computer's start-failure history is for instance admins only (owner, 2026-11-20; FIX-196; amends C-613, extends SEC-141).** `GET …/start-failures` gives the runner's wording and pod events only to instance admins; workspace owners and members get each failure's class and the member hint, as `GET …/sandbox` already does since P5-REFINE-4. | Owners keep failure detail (C-1059 as written) | The detail carries the same deployment facts (registry, tags, cluster addresses) SEC-141 keeps from workspace owners. |
| D-425 | **The organization's agents list shows each agent's state in words and its last use (owner, 2026-11-20; confirms C-1090, P5-REFINE-6 for P5-REFINE-17 org-6).** Rows say Working, Asleep, Needs a key, Paused or Ready and "used … ago", from C-1090's `state` and `last_active_at`. | Ready/Paused only (P5-REFINE-17's interim) | The mockup shows it; an admin scanning the organization needs to see which agents work. |
| D-426 | **Model sizes use the new words everywhere, the admin pages included (owner, 2026-11-20; P5-REFINE-12, -18).** The rename P5-REFINE-12 applied to the agent's pages (Large/Medium/Small) also applies to Administration › AI models and every other page that names a model size. | Agent pages only | One vocabulary (D-407). |
| D-427 | **The Balanced preset asks before changes at the organization level (owner, 2026-11-20; P5-REFINE-18).** The instance has no "ask before changing data" setting, so the instance-level Balanced preset changes only what the instance owns (key scopes, outside agents) and its card says so. Its "ask before an agent changes data" part is applied by the organization-level Balanced preset, as an organization rule that asks before write actions (the existing Ask rule shape, no new API). Open stays the registry defaults. | A new instance-wide ask-before-writes setting (backend work); Balanced ≈ Open at instance level with nothing more | Gives Balanced its meaning with the rules that already exist; organizations are where policy is set. |
| D-428 | **Five more built-in apps after the UX work: Grafana, Zoom, ServiceNow, Foreman, Rundeck (owner request 2026-11-20; coordinator design from that day's research; `docs/tasks/P5-CATALOG.md`).** (a) Each is an ordinary built-in package over the product's REST API; none is an MCP-only app (`24 §12`, D-172(b)): every one has a complete HTTP API. Vendor and community MCP servers (grafana/mcp-grafana, ServiceNow MCP Server Console and echelon-ai-labs/servicenow-mcp, Zoom's hosted MCP, theforeman/foreman-mcp-server, PagerDuty's Rundeck MCP) were read only as evidence of which operations agents use. (b) Generic packages in `plugins/builtin/`, not CERN addons; a CERN-specific sign-in (for example Foreman "Judy" behind single sign-on) would be a CERN addon method later. (c) Prometheus, Mimir and Loki are reached through Grafana's datasource proxy with GET-only tools for named backend paths; no separate Prometheus/Mimir/Alertmanager app in this wave. (d) OpenSearch (needs a port on instances, often `:9200`) and OpenStack (needs service-catalog routing across hosts and ports, D-172(c)) wait for their own specs. (e) Endpoints that create or reveal credentials are never tools (SEC-145). (f) The wave starts after P5-REFINE-1..20 and P5-POLISH-1..11 have merged; one `package` lane in sequence (Grafana → Foreman → Rundeck → ServiceNow → Zoom) or two with the shared one-line files rebased. | Attaching vendor MCP servers to the harness; a Prometheus/Mimir app beside Grafana; putting Foreman in `aurora-addons-cern`; including OpenSearch and OpenStack now | REST covers every app and keeps grants, approvals and the request log meaningful per operation; Grafana already holds the metrics backends' credentials and tenant headers; the two deferred apps need core changes first. |
| D-429 | **`grafana` package (P5-GRAFANA).** Instance-bound, no default host, base paths allowed; one method, a service-account token as bearer under `/api` (Viewer for reads, Editor for the write tools). Reads are GET; datasource-proxy tools only for named Prometheus/Mimir (`api/v1/query`, `query_range`, `series`, `labels`, `label/*/values`, `metadata`, `rules`, `alerts`, `query_exemplars`) and Loki paths, every other proxy path untooled. Writes with approvals: annotation, dashboard save, silence create/end, and `POST /api/ds/query` as a held read (superseded by D-462: a read tool). Refused: token, service-account and API-key creation, user/org admin, datasource create/update/delete, dashboard deletion, plugin install. | Basic auth with a person's password; legacy API keys; a generic proxy tool for any backend path | Single sign-on instances have no usable password; a datasource proxy forwards any method, so only named read paths may be tools. |
| D-430 | **`zoom` package (P5-ZOOM).** SaaS (`api.zoom.us/v2`). Two methods: personal OAuth through a General app the account's organization creates and registers in Aurora (`client_auth: basic`, refresh tokens rotate), and an account-wide Server-to-Server app whose one-hour token a `token.mint` hook gets with `grant_type=account_credentials` (egress `zoom.us` only). Granular scopes, read ones by default. Tools: users, meetings, past meetings and participants, cloud recordings and transcripts; writes: create, change and cancel a meeting. Recording downloads only on documented, bounded hosts and paths, never `*.zoom.us` as a whole. Refused: recording deletion, user create/delete, settings changes, app/token management. Team Chat, Phone, Webinars, Rooms: not in this package. | One Aurora-wide Zoom Marketplace app (needs Zoom's review and a publisher); the deprecated JWT app type; Zoom's MCP server for semantic search | The account owner controls its own app; the hook reuses the `token.mint` shape of `aws-keys`. |
| D-431 | **`servicenow` package (P5-SERVICENOW).** Instance-bound, no default host; credentials only under `/api`. Method 1 (preferred): OAuth authorization code with an instance-bound client the instance admin registers (`/oauth_auth.do`, `/oauth_token.do`), so requests run as the person and ServiceNow's row and field rules apply to them. Method 2: an integration account with basic auth, labelled as acting as that account. Reads: incidents, requests, request items, catalog tasks, problems, changes, journal entries, knowledge, the service catalog, users and groups, CMDB, attachments, aggregates on those tables; every readable table is named, with no catch-all `GET /api/now/table/*` (matching has no exclusions, so it would classify credential tables as reads, SEC-145); unversioned and versioned Table API paths. Writes: open and update an incident, comment on a request item, order a catalog item, attach a file. Refused: record deletion, writes to unlisted and `sys_*` tables, OAuth and credential tables, Import Set loads. | Basic auth only; the instance's MCP Server Console (needs Now Assist licences, runs Now Assist skills); a generic table-write tool | Per-person OAuth is the only way ServiceNow's per-user rules hold; the write list is what people ask agents to do. |
| D-432 | **`foreman` package (P5-FOREMAN).** Instance-bound, no default host; username plus personal access token as basic auth under `/api` and the `foreman_puppet` plugin's API prefix. Reads: hosts (status, facts, parameters with hidden values never revealed, reports, interfaces; the ENC output is refused because it carries hidden values in clear), hostgroups, Puppet environments, classes and smart class parameters, config reports, fact values, infrastructure lookups, audits, job templates and invocations. Writes: run a remote execution job (the approval shows the exact target), cancel a job, set a host parameter. Refused: host deletion, power and boot, rebuild, host and hostgroup edits, personal access token creation, user/role/filter edits, Katello content-view publish/promote. | A password instead of a token; host edits and power actions as approved writes; leaving remote execution out | A token can expire and be revoked; remote execution is the one action people want from an agent and Foreman's own MCP server also gates it. |
| D-433 | **`rundeck` package (P5-RUNDECK).** Instance-bound, no default host, base paths allowed (served on 443; `:4440` needs the instance-port change); a user API token in `X-Rundeck-Auth-Token` under `/api`, never the `authtoken` query parameter; tools match any API version (`/api/*/…`). Reads: system and user info, projects, jobs and their definitions, forecasts and history, executions with state and paged output, nodes. Writes: run a defined job (the approval shows the options and node filter as sent), retry an execution, abort an execution. Refused: ad hoc command/script/URL runs, job import/create/delete, project admin, ACL edits, API token endpoints, key storage. Covers Rundeck Community and Runbook Automation (same API). | Ad hoc commands as an approved write; pinning one API version | Ad hoc runs are arbitrary code on nodes outside any job definition the operators reviewed; servers differ in their highest version. |
| D-434 | **SSH keepalives count as activity, within the existing 4 h limits (owner 2026-11-21, P5-REFINE-20 pass 3; FIX-199).** An SSH client's keepalives (`keepalive@openssh.com` and other no-op global or channel requests) count as session activity: they reset the idle timeout (`SSH_IDLE_TIMEOUT`, 600 s) as channel bytes do, and they keep the computer's SSH-held wakefulness alive (`SSH_AWAKE_GRACE` restarts). No new limit: a keepalive-only session still ends at `SSH_MAX_LIFETIME` (4 h), and the computer's SSH-held wakefulness still stops at `SSH_AWAKE_CAP` (4 h per boot). The SSH page says it in words. **Implemented as (FIX-199, coordinator 2026-11-21):** russh 0.65.2 never shows its handler a client's `keepalive@openssh.com`, and patching russh stays rejected (D-356), so the terminator counts any byte read from the client's TCP connection as activity (never written bytes; the clock is touched at most once per second), turns off russh's own server keepalive (its reply would make every live client active) and detects dead peers with TCP keepalive on the accepted socket (idle 30 s, interval 10 s, 3 probes, about 60 s); the wake is the API's session heartbeat stamping `ssh_active_until` (at most one write per half grace). | Keepalives never count (a quiet session with a long-running command is cut after 10 min although its client is alive); a separate 6 h keepalive-only limit (never reached under the 4 h lifetime); raising the lifetime or the wake cap to 6 h. | A live client with a quiet command keeps its session and its computer; the existing 4 h maximum still ends a forgotten one. |
| D-435 | **Agent templates in phase 6 (owner, 2026-11-21; `docs/tasks/P6-TEMPLATES.md`; distribution and contents amended by D-456).** The owner proposed agent templates: an instance owner adds an external repository of ready-made agent setups (instructions, apps with their access, skills, schedules), and a person creates an agent from one, connects the accounts it needs and changes it afterwards as they like. Answers to the sketch's questions: (a) repositories are added by **instance admins only** in this version (organization-level repositories after P6-ORGTREE); (b) **no signing**: the admin's review and approval of each version is the trust gate; (c) evals in templates come later; (d) no policy rules in templates in this version; (e) an app entry may name alternatives (`any_of`), needed for calendars; (f) **Save as template** is in this version. | Organization-level repositories now; signed repositories like addon sources; templates carrying rules or evals now | The admin reads and approves every version and nothing in a template runs on the platform, so a key adds friction without adding a check; rules and organization sources wait for the features they depend on. |
| D-436 | **How agent templates work (coordinator, 2026-11-21, P6-TEMPLATES; C-1103..C-1119, C-1141, SEC-146; (b), (f) and (h) amended by D-457).** (a) A template is the agent export (D-413, `14 §Export and import an agent`) plus `template.yaml`; the built-in purpose templates stay in the web bundle and repository templates appear beside them (**More templates…**). (b) ~~One zip per version, its sha256 in the repository's `aurora-templates.yaml`~~ (replaced by D-457(b)); Aurora stores the parsed content at ingest, and the admin approves that content by its hash. (c) A template sets no access: its levels are preselections, capped by the account's limit and `custom_only`, tools the installed app lacks are dropped and said. (d) The model is always the installation's default and the harness its default, because both differ between installations. (e) Creation is one transaction through the existing writers; webhooks start paused, schedules run with the creator's authority. (f) Updates are offered per part, with a full diff (the parts: D-457(g)); parts a person changed are not ticked; memories and automations are a starting point and are never offered again; apps a new version adds are listed with a link to give access.
